Charter Ordinance No. Sixty-One (Repealed by Ch. Ord. No. Sixty-Six)

(Note: All or parts of this charter ordinance have been repealed. See notes at the end of the ordinance.)

A CHARTER ORDINANCE AMENDING AND REPEALING SECTION 2 OF CHARTER ORDINANCE NO. FIFTY-ONE PERTAINING TO THE POWERS OF MUNICIPAL COURT JUDGES AND PROVIDING SUBSTITUTE AND ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS ON THE SAME SUBJECT.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. Section 2 of Charter Ordinance No. Fifty-One of the City of Overland Park, Kansas, be and the same is hereby amended to read as follows:

SECTION 2. Municipal judges; powers and duties.

  1. a.
    The municipal judge shall have the power to administer oaths and enforce due obedience to all orders, rules and judgments made by him or her, and may fine or imprison for contempt committed in court or for failure to obey process issued by him or her, in the same manner and to the same extent as the district court
  2. b.
    The municipal judge shall have the power to hear and determine all cases properly brought before him or her, to grant continuances, to sentence those found guilty to a fine or confinement in jail, or both, to commit accused persons to jail in default of bond, to determine applications for parole, to release on probation, to grant time in which a fine may be paid, to correct a sentence, to suspend imposition of a sentence, to set aside a judgment, to permit time for post trial motions and to discharge accused persons.
  3. c.
    The municipal judge shall promptly make such reports and furnish the information requested by any departmental justice or the judicial administrator, in the manner and form prescribed by the supreme court.
  4. d.
    The municipal judge shall maintain a docket in which he or she shall enter every cause commenced before him or her. Said docket shall contain the names of the accused persons and complainant, the nature or character of the offense, the date of trial, the names of all witnesses sworn and examined, the finding of the court, the judgment and sentence, the date of payment, the date of issuing commitment, if any, and every other fact necessary to show the full proceedings in each case.
  5. e.
    All of the above-listed powers and duties of municipal judges, except those powers and duties set forth in subsection (c), may be modified, restricted, or expanded by ordinances duly enacted by the Governing Body.
  6. f.
    Court services officers.
    1. 1.
      The Administrative Judge of the Municipal Court is authorized to appoint court services officers, in such numbers and for such compensation that is in accordance with the annual budget adopted by the Governing Body. The court services officers shall serve under the direction and control of the Administrative Judge, who shall set the qualifications for appointment to that office consistent with the requirements of law. The Mayor may issue commissions and badges for the court services officers upon the recommendation of the Administrative Judge.
    2. 2.
      The duties of a court services officer shall include enforcing and serving court orders and processes, maintaining public order in the court operations and facilities, protecting the court and its officers in the exercise of their judicial functions, and executing all orders issued or made by lawful authority of the municipal court. A court services officer may, subject to the direction and control of the Administrative Judge as set forth in subsection (f)(1), exercise the power to make an arrest or otherwise take a person into custody, detain or otherwise restrain a person, carry firearms, concealed or otherwise, serve process, or exercise any other lawful power necessary to execute or enforce municipal court orders or perform their duties as specified in this subsection.

SECTION 2. Existing Section 2 of Charter Ordinance No. Fifty-One is hereby repealed.

SECTION 3. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in The Overland Park Sun, an official City newspaper.

SECTION 4. THIS IS A CHARTER ORDINANCE AND SHALL TAKE EFFECT 61 DAYS AFTER FINAL PUBLICATION UNLESS WITHIN 60 DAYS OF ITS FINAL PUBLICATION A PETITION SIGNED BY A NUMBER OF ELECTORS OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK EQUAL TO NOT LESS THAN TEN PERCENT OF THE NUMBER OF ELECTORS WHO VOTED AT THE LAST PRECEDING REGULAR CITY ELECTION SHALL BE FILED IN THE OFFICE OF THE CITY CLERK OF OVERLAND PARK DEMANDING AN ELECTION ON THE CHARTER ORDINANCE, IN WHICH CASE THE CHARTER ORDINANCE SHALL BECOME EFFECTIVE ONLY IF AND WHEN APPROVED BY A MAJORITY OF THE ELECTORS VOTING THEREON.

PASSED by the Governing Body not less than twothirds of the members elect voting in favor thereof the 1st day of March, 1993.

Ed Eilert, Mayor

Norma Moffet, City Clerk

Charter Ordinance No. Sixty-Four

(Note: All or parts of this charter ordinance have been repealed. See notes at the end of the ordinance.)

A CHARTER ORDINANCE EXEMPTING THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS, FROM THE PROVISIONS OF K.S.A. 12-194; RELATING TO PROHIBITION OF CERTAIN EXCISE TAXES; AND PROVIDING A SUBSTITUTE AND ADDITIONAL PROVISION AUTHORIZING THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS, TO LEVY AN EXCISE TAX ON THE ACT OF PLATTING REAL PROPERTY IN THE CITY, TO GRANT REBATES OF EXCISE TAXES PAID ON THE PRIVILEGE OF ENGAGING IN THE BUSINESS OF PLATTING REAL PROPERTY IN THE CITY FOR CONTRIBUTIONS PREVIOUSLY MADE FOR THOROUGHFARE IMPROVEMENTS, TO GRANT CREDITS AGAINST EXCISE TAXES PAID ON THE ACT OF PLATTING REAL PROPERTY IN THE CITY FOR CONTRIBUTIONS PREVIOUSLY MADE FOR THOROUGHFARE IMPROVEMENTS, TO GRANT EXEMPTIONS, AND TO PLEDGE THE REVENUES THEREFROM FOR IMPROVING THOROUGHFARES IN THE CITY.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. The City of Overland Park, Kansas, by the power vested in it by Article 12, Section 5 of the Constitution of the State of Kansas, hereby elects to exempt itself from and make inapplicable to it the provisions of K.S.A. 12-194, which provision reads as follows:

No city or county shall levy or impose an excise tax or a tax in the nature of an excise, other than a retailers' sales tax and a compensating use tax, upon the sale or transfer of personal or real property, or the use thereof, or the rendering of a service, but the provisions of this section shall not be construed as prohibiting any city from (a) contracting with a utility for a fixed charge based upon a percentage of gross receipts derived from the service permitted by grant, right, privilege or franchise to such utility; (b) imposing an occupational tax or license fee for the privilege of engaging in any business, trade, occupation or profession, or rendering or furnishing any service, but the determination of any such license fee shall not be based upon any amount the licensee has received from the sale or transfer of personal or real property, or for the rendering or furnishing of a service, or on the income of the licensee; or (c) levying any occupation tax or license fee imposed by such city prior to the effective date of this act. No license fee described in subsection (b) of this section shall be imposed upon any utility contracting with and subject to a charge, described in subsection (a) of this section, by such city.

Such referenced provisions are either enactments or a part thereof which are applicable to this city but are not uniformly applicable to all cities.

*SECTION 2. The City of Overland Park, Kansas, by the power vested in it by Article 12, Section 5 of the Constitution of the State of Kansas, hereby enacts the following substitute and additional provisions for those provisions exempted from in SECTION 1, above:

No city or county shall levy or impose an excise tax or a tax in the nature of an excise, other than a retailers' sales tax and a compensating use tax, upon the sale or transfer of personal or real property, or the use thereof, or the rendering of a service, but the provisions of this section shall not be construed as prohibiting any city from (a) contracting with a utility for a fixed charge based upon a percentage of gross receipts derived from the service permitted by grant, right, privilege or franchise to such utility; (b) imposing an occupational tax or license fee for the privilege of engaging in any business, trade, occupation or profession, or rendering or furnishing any service, but the determination of any such license fee shall not be based upon any amount the licensee has received from the sale or transfer of personal or real property, or for the rendering or furnishing of a service, or on the income of the licensee; (c) levying any occupation tax or license fee imposed by such city prior to the effective date of this act; or (d) levying an excise tax on the act of platting real property in the city, granting rebates of excise taxes paid on the privilege of engaging in the business of platting real property in the city for contributions previously made for thoroughfare improvements, granting credits against excise taxes paid on the act of platting real property in the city for contributions previously made for thoroughfare improvements, granting exemptions, and pledging the revenues therefrom for improving thoroughfares in the city. No license fee described in subsection (b) of this section shall be imposed upon any utility contracting with and subject to a charge, described in subsection (a) of this section, by such city.

SECTION 3. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in The Overland Park Sun, an official city newspaper.

SECTION 4. THIS IS A CHARTER ORDINANCE AND IT SHALL TAKE EFFECT 61 DAYS AFTER ITS FINAL PUBLICATION UNLESS WITHIN SIXTY DAYS OF ITS FINAL PUBLICATION A PETITION SIGNED BY A NUMBER OF ELECTORS OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK EQUAL TO NOT LESS THAN TEN PERCENT OF THE NUMBER OF ELECTORS WHO VOTED AT THE LAST PRECEDING REGULAR CITY ELECTION SHALL BE FILED IN THE OFFICE OF THE CITY CLERK OF OVERLAND PARK DEMANDING AN ELECTION ON THE CHARTER ORDINANCE, IN WHICH CASE THE CHARTER ORDINANCE SHALL BECOME EFFECTIVE ONLY IF AND WHEN APPROVED BY A MAJORITY OF THE ELECTORS VOTING THEREON.

PASSED by the governing body, not less than two-thirds of the members elect voting in favor thereof the 1st day of August, 1994.

Ed Eilert, Mayor

Charter Ordinance No. Sixty-Six

A CHARTER ORDINANCE EXEMPTING THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS FROM THE PROVISIONS OF K.S.A. 1995 SUPP. 12-4106; K.S.A. 12-4509 AND K.S.A. 12-4511 WHICH RELATE TO THE POWERS AND DUTIES OF MUNICIPAL COURT JUDGES AND THE SENTENCING AND PAROLE POWERS OF JUDGES IN THE MUNICIPAL COURT AND PROVIDING FOR THE ENACTMENT OF SUBSTITUTE AND ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS ON THE SAME SUBJECT THROUGH THE ENACTMENT OF AN ORDINARY ORDINANCE AND REPEALING EXISTING CHARTER ORDINANCES FIFTY-ONE AND SIXTY-ONE.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. The City of Overland Park, Kansas by power vested in it by Article 12, Section 5 of the Constitution of the State of Kansas, hereby elects to exempt itself from and make inapplicable to it provisions of K.S.A. 1995 Supp. 12-4106; K.S.A. 12-4509 and K.S.A. 12-4511 relating to the powers and duties of municipal court judges and the sentencing and parole powers of municipal court judges and providing for the enactment of substitute and additional provisions on the same subjects through the enactment of ordinary ordinance. Such referenced statutory provisions are applicable to this City but are not uniformly applicable to all cities.

A CHARTER ORDINANCE AMENDING AND REPEALING EXISTING SECTION 2 OF CHARTER ORDINANCE NO. FORTY-THREE RELATING TO THE RESPONSIBILITIES OF THE CITY MANAGER.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. Section 2 of Charter Ordinance No. Forty-Three of the City of Overland Park be and the same is hereby amended to read as follows:

SECTION 2. The City Manager shall be responsible for the administration of all of the affairs of the City. The City Manager shall see that the laws and ordinances of the City are enforced. The City Manager shall have the authority to appoint, promote, transfer, assign and demote, discipline, lay off, suspend, discharge, or remove all heads of departments, and all subordinate officers and employees of the City. All such appointments shall be made upon merit and fitness alone. Provided that, the City Manager may delegate at his or her discretion such authority to appoint, promote, transfer, assign and demote, discipline, lay off, suspend, discharge, or remove subordinate officers and employees of the City to department directors and division heads. In addition, the City Manager may, without notice, cause the affairs of any department or the conduct of any officer or employee to be examined. The City Manager shall prepare and submit the annual budget to the Governing Body and also keep the City fully advised as to the financial conditions and needs of the City. The City Manager may make recommendations to the Governing Body on all matters concerning the welfare of the City and shall have a seat, but no vote, in all of the public meetings of the Governing Body.

SECTION 2. Existing Section 2 of Charter Ordinance No. Forty-Three is hereby repealed.

SECTION 3. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in the Overland Park Sun, an official City newspaper.

SECTION 4. THIS IS A CHARTER ORDINANCE AND SHALL TAKE EFFECT 61 DAYS AFTER FINAL PUBLICATION UNLESS WITHIN 60 DAYS OF ITS FINAL PUBLICATION A PETITION SIGNED BY A NUMBER OF ELECTORS OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK EQUAL TO NOT LESS THAN TEN PERCENT OF THE NUMBER OF ELECTORS WHO VOTED AT THE LAST PRECEDING REGULAR CITY ELECTION SHALL BE FILED IN THE OFFICE OF THE CITY CLERK OF OVERLAND PARK DEMANDING AN ELECTION ON THE CHARTER ORDINANCE, IN WHICH CASE THE CHARTER ORDINANCE SHALL BECOME EFFECTIVE ONLY IF AND WHEN APPROVED BY

A MAJORITY OF THE ELECTORS VOTING THEREON.

Charter Ordinance No. Seventy (Repealed by Ch. Ord. No. Ninety-Nine)

(Note: All or parts of this charter ordinance have been repealed. See notes at the end of the ordinance.)

A CHARTER ORDINANCE EXEMPTING THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS, FROM THE PROVISIONS OF SUBPARAGRAPH (a) OF K.S.A. 12-1697 AND FROM THE PROVISIONS OF SUBPARAGRAPH (e) OF K.S.A. 12-1698, WHICH RELATE TO THE LEVY OF A TRANSIENT GUEST TAX, TO THE MAXIMUM RATE THEREOF, AND TO THE PURPOSES FOR WHICH TRANSIENT GUEST TAX REVENUES MAY BE SPENT; PROVIDING SUBSTITUTE AND ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS ON THE SAME SUBJECTS; AUTHORIZING THE ISSUANCE OF TRANSIENT GUEST TAX REVENUE BONDS WITHOUT AN ELECTION THEREON; AUTHORIZING EXPENDITURE OF TRANSIENT GUEST TAX REVENUES FOR THE PURPOSE OF PAYING THE PRINCIPAL AND INTEREST ON SALES TAX REVENUE BONDS, TRANSIENT GUEST TAX REVENUE BONDS OR OTHER BONDS ISSUED BY THE CITY FOR CERTAIN PURPOSES; AUTHORIZING A PLEDGE SOLELY AND ONLY OF TRANSIENT GUEST TAX REVENUES TO THE PAYMENT OF TRANSIENT GUEST TAX REVENUE BONDS WITHOUT AN ELECTION THEREON; AND REPEALING CHARTER ORDINANCE NOS. FORTY-TWO AND FORTY-SIX.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. The City of Overland Park, Kansas, by the power vested in it by Article 12, Section 5 of the Constitution of the State of Kansas, hereby elects to exempt itself from and make inapplicable to it the provisions of Subparagraph (a) of K.S.A. 12-1697 and the provisions of Subparagraph (e) of K.S.A. 12-1698, which relate to the levy of a transient guest tax, to the maximum rate thereof, and to the purposes for which transient guest tax revenues may be spent; hereby provides substitute and additional provisions on the same subjects as set forth herein; hereby authorizes the issuance of transient guest tax revenue bonds without an election thereon; hereby authorizes expenditure of transient guest tax revenues for the purpose of paying the principal and interest on sales tax revenue bonds, transient guest tax revenue bonds or other bonds issued by the City for certain purposes; and hereby authorizes a pledge solely and only of transient guest tax revenues to the payment of transient guest tax revenue bonds without an election thereon. Such referenced statutes are applicable to the City of Overland Park but are not uniformly applicable to all cities in Kansas.

*SECTION 2. The Governing Body of the City of Overland Park, Kansas, is hereby authorized to levy a transient guest tax at a rate not to exceed six percent (6%) upon the gross receipts derived from or paid by transient guests for sleeping accommodations, exclusive of charges for incidental services or facilities, in any hotel, motel or tourist court. The percentage of such tax shall be determined by the Governing Body and shall be specified in an ordinary ordinance authorizing the same.

SECTION 3. Revenues received by the City from the transient guest tax shall be expended solely and only for the purpose of promoting activities relating to conventions and tourism; for the purpose of paying all or a part of the cost of designing, acquiring, constructing, reconstructing, improving, equipping, furnishing, repairing, enlarging, remodeling, operating and maintaining capital projects relating to conventions and tourism, including, but not limited to real estate, buildings, improvements, furnishings, machinery and equipment for a convention center, exposition hall, public auditorium and related facilities; for the purpose of paying the principal and interest on sales tax revenue bonds, transient guest tax revenue bonds or other bonds issued by the City, the proceeds of which are used to design, acquire, construct, reconstruct, improve, equip, furnish, repair, enlarge and remodel such capital projects; and for the purpose of paying for economic development in an amount up to $50,000 per year. The revenues received by the City from the transient guest tax shall be credited to such fund or funds of the City and shall be apportioned among the above-listed purposes in an ordinary ordinance.

SECTION 4. The City of Overland Park is hereby authorized to issue transient guest tax revenue bonds for the purpose of paying all or part of the cost of designing, acquiring, constructing, reconstructing, improving, equipping, furnishing, repairing, enlarging or remodeling capital projects relating to conventions and tourism, including, but not limited to, real estate, buildings, improvements, furnishings, machinery and equipment for a convention center, exposition hall, public auditorium and related facilities, and to pledge solely and only transient guest tax revenues to the payment of the transient guest tax revenue bonds, all without an election thereon. In no event shall such bonds be a general obligation of the City of Overland Park, nor constitute an indebtedness of the City of Overland Park for which the faith and credit of the City of Overland Park is pledged, nor give rise to a charge against the general credit or taxing powers of the City of Overland Park, and such bonds shall so state on their face.

SECTION 5. Charter Ordinance No. Forty-Two and Charter Ordinance No. Forty-Six are hereby repealed.

SECTION 6. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in The Overland Park Sun, an official City newspaper.

SECTION 7. THIS IS A CHARTER ORDINANCE AND SHALL TAKE EFFECT 61 DAYS AFTER FINAL PUBLICATION UNLESS WITHIN 60 DAYS OF ITS FINAL PUBLICATION A PETITION SIGNED BY A NUMBER OF ELECTORS OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK EQUAL TO NOT LESS THAN TEN PERCENT OF THE NUMBER OF ELECTORS WHO VOTED AT THE LAST PRECEDING REGULAR CITY ELECTION SHALL BE FILED IN THE OFFICE OF THE CITY CLERK OF OVERLAND PARK DEMANDING AN ELECTION ON THE CHARTER ORDINANCE IN WHICH CASE THE CHARTER ORDINANCE SHALL BECOME EFFECTIVE ONLY IF AND WHEN APPROVED BY A MAJORITY OF THE ELECTORS VOTING THEREON.

PASSED by the Governing Body not less than two-thirds of the members elect voting in favor thereof the 6th day of October, 1997.

Ed Eilert, Mayor

Charter Ordinance No. Seventy-One

A CHARTER ORDINANCE EXEMPTING THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS, FROM SECTION 13-1017 OF THE KANSAS STATUTES ANNOTATED AND PROVIDING SUBSTITUTE PROVISIONS ON THE SAME SUBJECT; REPEALING CHARTER ORDINANCE THIRTY-THREE.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. The City of Overland Park, by the power invested in it by Article 12, Section 5 of the Constitution of the State of Kansas, hereby elects to and exempts itself from and makes inapplicable to it Section 13-1017 of the Kansas Statutes Annotated, said section applying only to cities of the first class, and provides substitute and additional provisions as hereinafter set forth.

SECTION 2. Estimate of cost of improvements; contracts; bids; bond issue, when.

Before the City of Overland Park undertakes the construction or reconstruction of any public improvement, including but not limited to, sidewalks, curbs, gutters, sewers, bridges, pavements, streets, highways, public grounds, public buildings and public facilities a detailed estimate of the cost of such improvement shall be made under oath by the city engineer, any department director or any other competent person appointed for such purposes by the Governing Body, and the estimate shall be submitted to the Governing Body for its action thereon.

In all cases where the estimated cost of the contemplated public improvement amounts to more than Fifty Thousand Dollars ($50,000), sealed proposals for the public improvement shall be invited by advertisement, published by the city clerk once in the official City newspaper, and the Governing Body shall let all such work by contract to the lowest and best responsible bidder. Notwithstanding the foregoing, the Governing Body reserves the right to refuse all or any part of any bid when it is felt that such action is in the best interest of the City.

If no qualified responsible person shall propose to enter into the contract at a price not exceeding the estimated cost, all bids may be rejected and the same proceedings as before repeated. In the alternative, if all bids exceed the estimated cost, the Governing Body may accept the bid and let the work by contract to the lowest and best responsible bidder; but if that cost is greater than seven percent over the estimated cost, the department director of the department letting the bids or his/her designee shall give a full explanation to the Governing Body of the reason for the discrepancy. In no case shall the City be liable for anything beyond the original contract price for doing the work or making the improvement.

Before any type of public improvement shall be commenced, the money to pay for the same must be available in the City treasury as provided by law; or provision may be made for the issuance of internal improvement bonds to pay for any such improvement as provided by law.

The bidding process herein set forth may be waived by an affirmative vote of a majority of the Governing Body, if the best interest of the City would be so served.

This section shall not apply to:

  1. a.
    any repair or maintenance work not amounting to substantial alteration, addition or change in any public improvement.
  2. b.
    the construction, reconstruction, repair or the maintenance of any public improvement by employees of the City .
  3. c.
    improvements financed by economic development revenue bonds issued pursuant to K.S.A. 12-1740, et seq..

SECTION 3. Charter Ordinance No. Thirty-Three is hereby repealed.

SECTION 4. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in the Overland Park Sun, an official City newspaper.

SECTION 5. THIS IS A CHARTER ORDINANCE AND SHALL TAKE EFFECT 61 DAYS AFTER FINAL PUBLICATION UNLESS WITHIN 60 DAYS OF ITS FINAL PUBLICATION A PETITION SIGNED BY A NUMBER OF ELECTORS OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK EQUAL TO NOT LESS THAN TEN PERCENT OF THE NUMBER OF ELECTORS WHO VOTED AT THE LAST PRECEDING REGULAR CITY ELECTION SHALL BE FILED IN THE OFFICE OF THE CITY CLERK OF OVERLAND PARK DEMANDING AN ELECTION ON THE CHARTER ORDINANCE, IN WHICH CASE THE CHARTER ORDINANCE SHALL BECOME EFFECTIVE ONLY IF AND WHEN APPROVED BY A MAJORITY OF THE ELECTORS VOTING THEREON.

PASSED by the Governing Body not less than two-thirds of the members elect voting in favor thereof the 2nd day of February, 1998.

Ed Eilert, Mayor

ATTEST:

Norma Moffet, City Clerk

APPROVED AS TO FORM:

Jane Neff-Brain

Senior Assistant City Attorney

Passed: 2/2/98

Published: 2/6/98

SECTION 2. Commencing January 1, 1999, the salary of each City Council Member shall be an amount not to exceed $10,200 per year, payable monthly.

SECTION 3. Commencing January 1, 1999, the City Council Member who serves as President of the Council also shall receive an additional amount not to exceed $1,800 per year, payable monthly while serving in that capacity.

SECTION 4. Commencing January 1, 1999, the salary of the Mayor shall be an amount not to exceed $20,400 per year, payable monthly.

SECTION 5. The procedure for determining the amount of the salary of each Council Member, the President of the Council and the Mayor, up to the not to exceed amount, shall be determined by resolution of the governing body.

SECTION 6. The not to exceed amount of the salaries of the Council Members, President of the Council and Mayor shall be reviewed at least once every four years.

SECTION 7. Charter Ordinance No. Sixty-Three is hereby repealed effective January 1, 1999.

SECTION 8. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in The Overland Park Sun, an official City newspaper.

Charter Ordinance No. Seventy-Six (Repealed by Ch. Ord. No. Eighty-Four)

(Note: All or parts of this charter ordinance have been repealed. See notes at the end of the ordinance.)

A CHARTER ORDINANCE CHANGING CITY ELECTIONS IN THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS, FROM PARTISAN TO NONPARTISAN ELECTIONS; CHANGING THE METHOD OF FILLING VACANCIES IN THE OFFICE OF COUNCIL MEMBER IN THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS, FROM A PARTISAN TO A NON-PARTISAN METHOD; EXEMPTING THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS, FROM K.S.A. 25-2113 AND 13-513; PROVIDING SUBSTITUTE AND ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS ON THE SAME SUBJECTS; REPEALING SECTION 6 OF CHARTER ORDINANCE NO. 34; REPEALING CHARTER ORDINANCE NO. 49 EFFECTIVE APRIL 7, 2003; AND SUBJECTING THIS CHARTER ORDINANCE TO A VOTE OF THE ELECTORS OF THE CITY.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. Statutes made inapplicable. The City of Overland Park, by the power vested in it by Article 12, Section 5 of the Constitution of the State of Kansas, hereby elects to exempt itself from and make inapplicable to it the provisions of K.S.A. 25-2113 and 13-513 and provide substitute and additional provisions as hereinafter set forth in this charter ordinance. Such referenced provisions are either enactments or a part thereof which are applicable to this city but are not applicable uniformly to all cities.

SECTION 2. Nonpartisan elections. City elections shall be nonpartisan. Laws applicable to elections occurring at the same time as city elections shall apply to city elections to the extent that the same are not in conflict with the provisions of Article 21 of Chapter 25 of the Kansas Statutes Annotated.

*SECTION 3. Date of primary city election; procedure for conduct of primary and general city elections.

  1. A.
    Date of primary city election. The primary election of the city officers shall be on the Tuesday preceding by five weeks the first Tuesday in April of every odd-numbered year.
  2. B.
    Procedure for conduct of primary city elections.
    1. 1.
      In 2005 and in each subsequent year in which the term of the mayor expires, if no more than two (2) candidates have filed for the office of mayor, both such candidates shall automatically be listed on the general city election ballot; but if more than two (2) candidates have filed for the office of mayor, a primary city election at large shall be held for the nomination of two (2) candidates for the office. Each qualified elector of the city shall be entitled to vote for one candidate. The two (2) candidates receiving the highest number of votes cast shall be placed on the general city election ballot as the candidates nominated for election as mayor.
    2. 2.
      In 2003 and in each subsequent year in which the term of a council member expires, if no more than two (2) candidates have filed for the office of council member to be filled, both such candidates shall automatically be listed on the general city election ballot; but if more than two (2) candidates have filed for the office of council member to be filled, a primary city election shall be held within each such ward for the nomination of two (2) candidates from that ward. Each qualified elector who is a resident of the ward shall be entitled to vote for one candidate from his or her ward. The two (2) candidates from each such ward receiving the highest number of votes cast shall be placed on the general city election ballot as the candidates for election from the ward.
  3. C.
    Procedure for conduct of general city elections.
    1. 1.
      In the general city election in 2005 and in each subsequent year in which the term of the mayor expires, all qualified electors of the city shall be entitled to vote on the election of the mayor. Of the candidate or candidates nominated or automatically listed for the office of mayor, the candidate receiving the highest number of votes in the general city election shall be elected as mayor.
    2. 2.
      In the general city election in 2003 and in each subsequent year in which the term of a council member expires, all qualified electors of the ward shall be entitled to vote on the election of the council member from that ward. Of the candidate or candidates nominated or automatically listed from each ward, the candidate receiving the highest number of votes in the general city election shall be elected as council member from the ward.

SECTION 4. Filling vacancies in office of mayor and council member. Effective on and after April 7, 2003:

  1. A.
    The mayor and council members shall remain in office until their successors have been duly elected and qualified. When there is a vacancy in the position of mayor, the president of the council, upon being qualified, shall become mayor. If such vacancy occurs before January 1 of an odd-numbered year leaving an unexpired term of more than two (2) years from said January 1, such successor shall serve in such position until a special election is held at the same time as the next regular city primary and general election. Such special election shall be held for the purpose of electing a replacement mayor to serve in such position for the remainder of the unexpired term. Such special election shall be held in the same manner as if the term were expiring and the term of the successor shall end upon election of a mayor for the unexpired term. In all other cases, the successor shall serve the remainder of the unexpired term.
  2. B.
    When the president of the council becomes mayor, there shall be a vacancy in such council member's position on the council. Whenever a vacancy occurs for any reason in the position of council member, a screening committee composed of the mayor, president of the council and chair of the finance, administration and economic development committee of the council shall recommend not to exceed two (2) persons as potential replacements to the council within thirty (30) days from the date the vacancy is declared to have occurred by the members of the council remaining after the vacancy. The remaining members of the council and the mayor shall so declare a vacancy to have occurred at the first regular council meeting following the date of occurrence of the vacancy. Upon receipt of such recommendation, the council, by majority vote of the remaining members thereof and the mayor, may appoint either recommended replacement or may reject both such recommended replacements. Should the remaining members of the council and the mayor reject both such recommended replacements, the screening committee shall make a second recommendation of not to exceed two (2) persons other than the two persons originally recommended, as replacements for council consideration within fifteen (15) days of such rejection. Upon receipt of the second recommendation, the council, by majority vote of the remaining members thereof and the mayor, may appoint either such recommended replacement or may reject both such recommended replacements. Should the remaining members of the council and the mayor reject both such recommended replacements, the council, by majority vote of the remaining members thereof and the mayor, shall appoint an elector qualified to fill the vacant position.
  3. C.
    If such vacancy occurs in the position of council member before January 1 of an odd-numbered year leaving an unexpired term of more than two (2) years from said January 1, such appointed replacement shall serve in such position until a special election is held at the same time as the next regular city primary and general election. Such special election shall be held for the purpose of electing a replacement to serve in such position for the remainder of the unexpired term. Such special election shall be held in the same manner as if the term were expiring and the term of the appointed member, if any, shall end upon election of a member for the unexpired term. In all other cases, the appointed replacement shall serve the remainder of the unexpired term.
  4. D.
    SECTION 5. Repeal of charter ordinances. Section 6 of Charter Ordinance No. 34 be and the same is hereby repealed and Charter Ordinance No. 49 of the City of Overland Park, Kansas, be and the same is hereby repealed effective April 7, 2003.

SECTION 6. Severability. It is hereby declared to be the intention of the Governing Body that the sections, subsections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this charter ordinance are severable, and if any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph, subsection or section of this charter ordinance shall be declared unconstitutional or otherwise invalid by the valid judgment or decree of any court of competent jurisdiction, such unconstitutionality or invalidity shall not affect any of the remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs, subsections and sections of this charter

ordinance, since the same would have been enacted by the Governing Body without the incorporation into this charter ordinance of any unconstitutional or otherwise invalid phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph, subsection or section.

SECTION 7. Publication of charter ordinance. This charter ordinance shall be published once each week for two (2) consecutive weeks in The Overland Park Sun, an official city newspaper.

SECTION 8. Election on charter ordinance. This is a charter ordinance and its effectiveness is hereby made subject to a vote of the electors of the City of Overland Park, Kansas. It shall take effect only if and when approved by a majority of the electors voting thereon.

PASSED BY THE GOVERNING BODY, NOT LESS THAN TWO-THIRDS OF THE MEMBERS-ELECT VOTING IN FAVOR THEREOF, THE 5TH DAY OF FEBRUARY, 2001.

Ed Eilert, Mayor

ATTEST:

Marian Cook, City Clerk

APPROVED AS TO FORM:

Robert J. Watson, City Attorney

Passed: 2/5/2001

Published: 2/7/2001

2/14/2001

Election: 4/3/2001

Effective: 4/6/2001

Charter Ordinance No. Seventy-Seven (Repealed by Ch. Ord. No. Eighty-Four)

(Note: All or parts of this charter ordinance have been repealed or did not take effect by operation of law. See notes at the end of the ordinance.)

A CHARTER ORDINANCE ADDING A SIXTH WARD IN THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS; REPEALING SECTIONS 4 AND 6 OF CHARTER ORDINANCE NUMBER THIRTY-FOUR AND REPEALING CHARTER ORDINANCE NUMBERS FORTY-SEVEN AND FIFTY-FOUR; AND SUBJECTING THIS CHARTER ORDINANCE TO A VOTE OF THE ELECTORS OF THE CITY.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

**SECTION 1. Composition of the Governing Body and eligibility for election thereto.

  1. A.
    Composition. The Governing Body shall consist of a mayor elected by the City as a whole and ten (10) council members elected by wards; provided, for purposes of the City elections in 2003 and thereafter, the Governing Body shall consist of a mayor elected by the City as a whole and twelve (12) council members elected by wards. Each ward shall be represented by two council members.
  2. B.
    Eligibility. The mayor and each council member shall be a citizen of the United States and a qualified elector of Overland Park, Kansas, and must reside in the City at the time of filing for election and thereafter for the duration of their terms. Council members from wards shall be residents of their respective wards at the time of filing for election and thereafter for the duration of their terms.

SECTION 2. Number of wards. The City shall be divided into five wards to be numbered one through five; provided, effective in December, 2002, the City shall be divided into six wards numbered one through six for purposes of the City elections held in 2003 and thereafter.

SECTION 3. Ward boundaries. Dividing lines between wards shall follow precinct boundaries established by the Johnson County Election Commissioner. The Governing Body of the City shall, by ordinance, adjust the ward boundaries to reflect approximately equal population effective in December of 2002. The Governing Body of the City may likewise adjust ward boundaries effective in December every two years thereafter but shall adjust the boundaries effective in December every four years thereafter. The Governing Body shall certify the boundaries so adjusted to the Johnson County Election Commissioner.

SECTION 4. Date of primary City election; procedure for conduct of primary and general City elections.

  1. A.
    If Charter Ordinance No. Seventy-Six is approved by a majority of the electors on April 3, 2001, Section 3 of Charter Ordinance No. Seventy-Six is hereby amended to read as follows:
    1. 1.
      Date of primary City election. The primary election of the City officers shall be on the Tuesday preceding by five weeks the first Tuesday in April of every odd-numbered year.
    2. 2.
      Procedure for conduct of primary City elections.
      1. a.
        In 2005 and in each subsequent year in which the term of the mayor expires, if no more than two (2) candidates have filed for the office of mayor, both such candidates shall automatically be listed on the general City election ballot; but if more than two (2) candidates have filed for the office of mayor, a primary City election at large shall be held for the nomination of two (2) candidates for the office. Each qualified elector of the City shall be entitled to vote for one (1) candidate. The two (2) candidates receiving the highest number of votes cast shall be placed on the general City election ballot as the candidates nominated for election as mayor.
      2. b.
        In 2003 and in each subsequent year in which the term of a council member expires, if no more than two (2) candidates have filed for the office of council member to be filled, both such candidates shall automatically be listed on the general City election ballot; but if more than two (2) candidates have filed for the office of council member to be filled, a primary City election shall be held within each such ward for the nomination of two (2) candidates from that ward. Each qualified elector who is a resident of the ward shall be entitled to vote for one candidate from his or her ward. The two (2) candidates from each such ward receiving the highest number of votes cast shall be placed on the general City election ballot as the candidates nominated for election from the ward; provided, in Ward Six in 2003 the two (2) Ward Six council positions shall be designated as Position No. 1 and Position No. 2 and both council positions shall be filled. A candidate may file for either Position No. 1 or Position No. 2, but not for both positions. If no more than two (2) candidates have filed for either or both such Ward Six positions, the candidates for such Ward Six position or positions shall automatically be listed on the general City election ballot; but if more than two (2) candidates have filed for either or both of the Ward Six positions, a primary City election shall be held for the nomination of two (2) candidates for such Ward Six position or positions. Each qualified elector who is a resident of Ward Six shall be entitled to vote for one (1) candidate for each Ward Six position. The two (2) candidates for each Ward Six position receiving the highest number of votes shall be placed on the general City election ballot as the candidates nominated for election to that Ward Six position.
    3. 3.
      Procedure for conduct of general City elections.
      1. a.
        In the general City election in 2005 and in each subsequent year in which the term of the mayor expires, all qualified electors of the City shall be entitled to vote on the election of the mayor. Of the candidate or candidates nominated or automatically listed for the office of mayor, the candidate receiving the highest number of votes in the general City election shall be elected as mayor.
      2. b.
        In the general City election in 2003 and in each subsequent year in which the term of a council member expires, all qualified electors of the ward shall be entitled to vote on the election of the council member from that ward. Of the two (2) candidates nominated or automatically listed from each ward, the candidate receiving the highest number of votes in the general City election shall be elected as council member from the ward; provided, in the general City election of 2003, all qualified electors of Ward Six shall be entitled to vote on the election of one council member for each Ward Six position. Of the candidate or candidates nominated or automatically listed for each Ward Six position, the candidate receiving the highest number of votes shall be elected to that Ward Six position.
  2. B.
    *If Charter Ordinance No. Seventy-Six is not approved by a majority of the electors on April 3, 2001, the following provisions shall take effect:
    1. 1.
      Date of primary election. The primary election of the City officers shall be on the Tuesday preceding by five weeks the first Tuesday in April of every odd-numbered year.
    2. 2.
      Procedure for conduct of primary City elections.
      1. a.
        In 2005 and in each subsequent year in which the term of the mayor expires, if no more than one (1) candidate has filed for the office of mayor from any officially recognized political party, such candidate shall automatically be listed on the general City election ballot as the candidate from that party; but if more than one (1) candidate has filed for the office of mayor from any officially recognized political party in Kansas entitled to have its candidate nominated by a primary election, a primary City election at large shall be held for the nomination of a candidate for the office from that party. Each qualified elector of the City who is eligible to vote in that party’s partisan primary under the election laws of the State of Kansas shall be entitled to vote for one (1) candidate. The candidate from that party receiving the highest number of votes cast shall be placed on the general City election ballot as the candidate from that party nominated for election as mayor. If more than one (1) candidate has filed for the office of mayor from any officially recognized political party other than one entitled to have its candidate nominated by a primary election, the nominee of that party shall be chosen by a delegate or mass convention. All independent candidates for the office of mayor shall automatically be listed on the general City election ballot.
      2. b.
        In 2003 and in each subsequent year in which the term of a council member expires, if no more than one (1) candidate from any officially recognized political party has filed for the office of council member to be filled, such candidate shall automatically be listed on the general election ballot as the candidate from that party; but if more than one (1) candidate from any officially recognized political party in Kansas entitled to have its candidate nominated by a primary election has filed for the office of council member to be filled, a primary City election shall be held within each such ward for the nomination of a candidate from that party from that ward. Each qualified elector who is a resident of the ward and who is eligible to vote in that party’s partisan primary under the election laws of the state of Kansas, shall be entitled to vote for one (1) candidate from his or her ward. The party candidate from each such ward who receives the highest number of votes cast shall be placed on the general City election ballot as the candidate nominated for election from that party from that ward; provided, in Ward Six in 2003 the two (2) Ward Six council positions shall be designated as Position No. 1 and Position No. 2 and both council positions shall be filled. A candidate may file for either Position No. 1 or Position No. 2, but not for both positions. If no more than one (1) candidate has filed for either or both of the Ward Six positions from any officially recognized political party, such candidate shall automatically be listed on the general election ballot as the candidate from that party for that position; but if more than one (1) candidate from any officially recognized political party in Kansas entitled to have its candidate nominated by a primary election has filed for either or both Ward Six positions, a primary City election shall be held for the nomination of one (1) candidate from that party for that Ward Six position. Each qualified elector who is a resident of Ward Six and who is eligible to vote in a partisan primary under the election laws of the State of Kansas shall be entitled to vote for one (1) candidate for each Ward Six position. The party candidate for each Ward Six position who receives the highest number of votes shall be placed on the general City election ballot as the candidate for election from that party for that Ward Six position. If more than one (1) candidate has filed for the office of council member from any officially recognized political party other than one entitled to have its candidate nominated by a primary election, the nominee of that party shall be chosen by a delegate or mass convention. All independent candidates for the office of council member shall automatically be listed on the general City election ballot.
    3. 3.
      Procedure for conduct of general City elections.
      1. a.
        In the general City election in 2005 and in each subsequent year in which the term of the mayor expires, all qualified electors of the City shall be entitled to vote on the election of the mayor. Of the candidate or candidates nominated or automatically listed for the office of mayor, the candidate receiving the highest number of votes in the general City election shall be elected as mayor
      2. b.
        In the general City election in 2003 and in each subsequent year in which the term of a council member expires, all qualified electors of the ward shall be entitled to vote on the election of the council member from that ward. Of the candidates nominated from each ward or listed automatically on the general City election ballot from that ward, the candidate receiving the highest number of votes in the general City election shall be elected as council member from the ward; provided, in the general City election of 2003, all qualified electors of Ward Six shall be entitled to vote on the election of one council member for each Ward Six position. Of the candidate or candidates nominated or listed automatically on the general City election ballot for each Ward Six position, the candidate receiving the highest number of votes in the general City election shall be elected to that Ward Six position.

SECTION 5. Terms of office.

  1. A.
    At the primary and general City elections held in 2003 all council member positions which were last filled by election in 1999 shall be filled by election, and those persons so elected in 2003 shall succeed to those council member positions. Also, at the primary and general City elections held in 2003 both council positions in Ward Six shall be filled by election. Those persons elected as council members in 2003 shall hold their positions for a term of four (4) years and until their successors are duly elected and qualified; provided, the candidate elected to council Position No. 1 in Ward Six in 2003 shall hold the position for an initial term of two (2) years.
  2. B.
    At the primary and general City elections held in 2005 the position of mayor and all council member positions which were last filled by election in 2001 and the Council member position in Ward Six filled by election to a two year term in 2003 shall be filled by election, and those persons so elected in 2005 shall succeed to those mayor and council member positions. Those persons elected as mayor and council members in 2005, and in subsequent elections, shall hold their positions for a term of four (4) years and until their successors are duly elected and qualified.

SECTION 6. Council president, election of, powers and duties. On the second Monday after the first Tuesday in the month of April of each year, the council shall elect by six (6) affirmative votes cast by signed written ballot, provided that abstentions and blanks shall not be counted as votes cast, one of its members as president of the council who, in the absence or disability of the mayor shall become acting mayor; provided, beginning in the month of April of 2003 the president of the council shall be elected by seven (7) affirmative votes. Such council member shall retain all his voting rights and other prerogatives as council member while acting as mayor.

SECTION 7. General powers and duties of the Governing Body; voting.

  1. A.
    General Powers and duties. All powers of the City shall be vested in the governing body, except as the Constitution and Statutes of Kansas or this or other charter ordinances may provide otherwise. The governing body shall provide for the performance of all duties and obligations imposed on the City by law.
  2. B.
    Voting.
    1. 1.
      Any ordinance shall be passed by an affirmative vote of six (6) council members unless a greater number of votes are specifically required by another provision of law; provided, that where the number of favorable votes is one less than required, the mayor shall have the power to cast the deciding vote in favor of the ordinance; and further provided that beginning on April 7, 2003, an ordinance shall be passed by an affirmative vote of seven (7) council members.
    2. 2.
      Any resolution, motion or any other action of the governing body except charter ordinances, upon which the mayor's vote shall be counted on every occasion, shall be passed at a meeting at which a quorum is present if more than half of the votes cast by council members are affirmative votes; provided, that abstentions shall not be counted as votes cast and where the number of affirmative votes is one less than required, the mayor shall have the power to cast the deciding vote in favor of the resolution, motion or other action.

SECTION 8. Repeal of charter ordinances. That Sections 4 and 6 of Charter Ordinance Number Thirty-Four, and Charter Ordinance Numbers Forty-Seven and Fifty-Four of the City of Overland Park, Kansas, be and the same are hereby repealed.

SECTION 9. Severability. It is hereby declared to be the intention of the governing body that the sections, subsections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this charter ordinance are severable, and if any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph, subsection or section of this charter ordinance shall be declared unconstitutional or otherwise invalid by the valid judgment or decree of any court of competent jurisdiction, such unconstitutionality or invalidity shall not affect any of the remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs, subsections and sections of this charter ordinance, since the same would have been enacted by the governing body without the incorporation into this charter ordinance of any unconstitutional or otherwise invalid phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph, subsection or section.

SECTION 10. Publication of charter ordinance. This charter ordinance shall be published once each week for two (2) consecutive weeks in The Overland Park Sun, an official City newspaper.

SECTION 11. Election on charter ordinance. This is a charter ordinance and its effectiveness is hereby made subject to a vote of the electors of the City of Overland Park, Kansas. It shall take effect only if approved by a majority of the electors voting thereon.

PASSED BY THE GOVERNING BODY, NOT LESS THAN TWO-THIRDS OF THE MEMBERS-ELECT VOTING IN FAVOR THEREOF, THE 5TH DAY OF FEBRUARY, 2001.

Ed Eilert, Mayor

ATTEST:

Marian Cook, City Clerk

APPROVED AS TO FORM:

Robert J. Watson, City Attorney

Charter Ordinance No. Seventy-Eight (Repealed by Ch. Ord. No. Eighty-Four)

(Note: All or parts of this charter ordinance have been repealed. See notes at the end of the ordinance.)

A CHARTER ORDINANCE RELATING TO THE FORM OF GOVERNMENT OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS; CONCERNING THE POWERS AND DUTIES OF THE MAYOR AND THE CITY MANAGER; EXEMPTING THE CITY FROM K.S.A. 13-506; REPEALING CHARTER ORDINANCE NO. SIXTY-EIGHT AND EXISTING SECTION 8 OF CHARTER ORDINANCE NO. THIRTY-FOUR.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. The City of Overland Park, by the power invested in it by Article 12, Section 5 of the Constitution of the State of Kansas, hereby elects to and exempts itself from and makes inapplicable to it K.S.A. 13-506, said section applying only to cities of the first class.

SECTION 2. POWERS AND DUTIES OF THE MAYOR

The Mayor shall be the titular head of the City and shall preside at all meetings of the Council. He shall sign all ordinances and resolutions passed by the Council; Provided, that he shall have the power to veto any ordinance or resolution passed by the Council, except the Mayor shall have no power to veto a Charter Ordinance, or appointments by the Council for vacancies on the Council. Any ordinance or resolution vetoed by the Mayor may be passed over the veto by the vote of twothirds of the whole number of the Councilmemberselect, notwithstanding the veto; and if the Mayor has failed to sign or veto such ordinance or resolution by the first regular meeting following the adoption of such ordinance or resolution, the same shall take effect without his signature. It shall be the duty of the Mayor to sign all contracts and agreements authorized by the Governing Body, except those contracts and agreements designated by the Governing Body to be entered into and signed by the City Manager or his/her designee. He shall represent the City Governing Body at all official City functions and shall be the official representative of the Governing Body at all meetings, conferences and negotiations relating to policy matters involving other governmental units and shall recommend Council action relating thereto. He shall be the official representative of the Governing Body at all conferences and meetings with citizens' groups and organizations interested in promoting the welfare and development of the City and shall recommend Council action relative thereto. He may annually communicate to the Council in writing such information as he may possess with reference to the condition of the City, and recommend such measures as he may deem necessary and expedient. He may inform the Council concerning questions of policy and may make policy recommendations to the Council.

SECTION 3. POWERS AND DUTIES OF THE CITY MANAGER

The City Manager shall be responsible for the administration of all of the affairs of the City. The City Manager shall see that the laws and ordinances of the City are enforced. The City Manager shall have the authority to appoint, promote, transfer, assign and demote, discipline, lay off, suspend, discharge, or remove all heads of departments, and all subordinate officers and employees of the City. All such appointments shall be made upon merit and fitness alone.

Provided that, the City Manager may delegate at his or her discretion such authority to appoint, promote, transfer, assign and demote, discipline, lay off, suspend, discharge, or remove subordinate officers and employees of the City to department directors and division heads. In addition, the City Manager may, without notice, cause the affairs of any department or the conduct of any officer or employee to be examined. The City Manager shall prepare and submit the annual budget to the Governing Body and also keep the City fully advised as to the financial conditions and needs of the City. The City Manager may make recommendations to the Governing Body on all matters concerning the welfare of the City and shall have a seat, but no vote, in all of the public meetings of the Governing Body. The City Manager, or his/her designee, may enter into, execute, approve and accept, on behalf of the City, all contracts, agreements and other documents as designated by the Governing Body.

SECTION 4. Charter Ordinance No. Sixty-Eight and existing Section 8 of Charter Ordinance No. Thirty-Four are hereby repealed.

SECTION 6. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in the Overland Park Sun, an official City newspaper.

SECTION 7. THIS IS A CHARTER ORDINANCE AND SHALL TAKE EFFECT 61 DAYS AFTER FINAL PUBLICATION UNLESS WITHIN 60 DAYS OF ITS FINAL PUBLICATION A PETITION SIGNED BY A NUMBER OF ELECTORS OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK EQUAL TO NOT LESS THAN TEN PERCENT OF THE NUMBER OF ELECTORS WHO VOTED AT THE LAST PRECEDING REGULAR CITY ELECTION SHALL BE FILED IN THE OFFICE OF THE CITY CLERK OF OVERLAND PARK DEMANDING AN ELECTION ON THE CHARTER ORDINANCE, IN WHICH CASE THE CHARTER ORDINANCE SHALL BECOME EFFECTIVE ONLY IF AND WHEN APPROVED BY A MAJORITY OF THE ELECTORS VOTING THEREON.

PASSED by the Governing Body not less than twothirds of the members elect voting in favor thereof the 5th day of March, 2001.

Ed Eilert, Mayor

ATTEST:

Marian Cook, City Clerk

APPROVED AS TO FORM:

Jane NeffBrain

Senior Assistant City Attorney

Charter Ordinance No. Eighty (Repealed by Charter Ord. No. Eighty-Eight)

(Note: All or parts of this charter ordinance have been repealed. See notes at the end of the ordinance.)

A CHARTER ORDINANCE EXEMPTING THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS, FROM SECTION 12-1036e OF THE KANSAS STATUTES ANNOTATED; PROVIDING SUBSTITUTE PROVISIONS ON THE SALARIES OF THE MAYOR AND CITY COUNCIL MEMBERS; AND REPEALING CHARTER ORDINANCE NO. SEVENTY-TWO.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. The City of Overland Park, Kansas, a Mayor-Council-City Manager city of the first class, by the power vested in it by Article 12, Section 5 of the Constitution of the State of Kansas, hereby elects to and does exempt itself from and make inapplicable to it, Section 12-1036e of the Kansas Statutes Annotated, which law applies only to Mayor-Council-City Manager cities of the first class, and to provide substitute provisions as herein provided.

SECTION 2. Commencing January 1, 2002, the salary of each City Council Member shall be an amount not to exceed $12,000 per year, payable monthly.

SECTION 3. Commencing January 1, 2002, the City Council Member who serves as President of the Council also shall receive an additional amount not to exceed $2,400 per year, payable monthly while serving in that capacity.

SECTION 4. Commencing January 1, 2002, the salary of the Mayor shall be an amount not to exceed $24,000 per year, payable monthly.

SECTION 5. The procedure for determining the amount of the salary of each Council Member, the President of the Council and the Mayor, up to the not to exceed amount, shall be determined by resolution of the governing body.

SECTION 6. The not to exceed amount of the salaries of the Council Members, President of the Council and Mayor shall be reviewed at least once every four years.

SECTION 7. Charter Ordinance No. Seventy-Two is hereby repealed effective January 1, 2002.

SECTION 8. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in The Overland Park Sun, an official City newspaper.

SECTION 9. THIS IS A CHARTER ORDINANCE AND SHALL TAKE EFFECT 61 DAYS AFTER FINAL PUBLICATION UNLESS WITHIN 60 DAYS OF ITS FINAL, PUBLICATION A PETITION-SIGNED BY A NUMBER OF ELECTORS OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK EQUAL TO NOT LESS THAN TEN PERCENT OF THE NUMBER OF ELECTORS WHO VOTED AT THE LAST PRECEDING REGULAR CITY ELECTION SHALL BE FILED IN THE OFFICE OF THE CITY CLERK OF OVERLAND PARK DEMANDING AN ELECTION ON THE CHARTER ORDINANCE IN WHICH CASE THE CHARTER ORDINANCE SHALL BECOME EFFECTIVE ONLY IF AND WHEN APPROVED BY A MAJORITY OF THE ELECTORS VOTING THEREON.

PASSED by the Governing Body not less than two-thirds of the members elect voting in favor thereof the 13th day of August, 2001.

Ed Eilert, Mayor

City Clerk

Charter Ordinance No. Eighty-One (Repealed by Charter Ord. No. Eighty-Eight)

(Note: All or parts of this charter ordinance have been repealed. See notes at the end of the ordinance.)

A CHARTER ORDINANCE AMENDING AND REPEALING EXISTING SECTION 1 OF CHARTER ORDINANCE NO. SEVENTY-SEVEN RELATING TO THE REQUIREMENT THAT COUNCIL MEMBERS BE RESIDENTS OF THEIR WARDS FOR THE DURATION OF THEIR TERMS.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. Section 1 of Charter Ordinance No. Seventy-Seven of the City of Overland Park be and the same is hereby amended to read as follows:

SECTION 1. Composition of the Governing Body and eligibility for election thereto.

  1. A.
    Composition. The Governing Body shall consist of a mayor elected by the City as a whole and ten (10) council members elected by wards; provided, for purposes of the City elections in 2003 and thereafter, the Governing Body shall consist of a mayor elected by the City as a whole and twelve (12) council members elected by wards. Each ward shall be represented by two council members.
  2. B.
    Eligibility. The mayor and each council member shall be a citizen of the United States and a qualified elector of Overland Park, Kansas, and must reside in the City at the time of filing for election and thereafter for the duration of their terms. Council members from wards shall be residents of their respective wards at the time of filing for election and thereafter for the duration of their terms, unless removal from the ward is the result of reapportionment.

SECTION 2. Existing Section 1 of Charter Ordinance No. Seventy-Seven is hereby repealed.

SECTION 3. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in The Overland Park Sun, an official City newspaper.

SECTION 4. THIS IS A CHARTER ORDINANCE AND SHALL TAKE EFFECT 61 DAYS AFTER FINAL PUBLICATION UNLESS WITHIN 60 DAYS OF ITS FINAL PUBLICATION A PETITION SIGNED BY A NUMBER OF ELECTORS OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK EQUAL TO NOT LESS THAN TEN PERCENT OF THE NUMBER OF ELECTORS WHO VOTED AT THE LAST PRECEDING REGULAR CITY ELECTION SHALL BE FILED IN THE OFFICE OF THE CITY CLERK OF OVERLAND PARK DEMANDING AN ELECTION ON THE CHARTER ORDINANCE, IN WHICH CASE THE CHARTER ORDINANCE SHALL BECOME EFFECTIVE ONLY IF AND WHEN APPROVED BY A MAJORITY OF THE ELECTORS VOTING THEREON.

PASSED by the Governing Body not less than twothirds of the members elect voting in favor thereof the 9th day of September, 2002.

Ed Eilert, Mayor

Charter Ordinance No. Eighty-Two (Repealed by Chapter 201 of the 2005 Session Laws of Kansas)

(Note: All or parts of this charter ordinance have been repealed. See notes at the end of the ordinance.)

A CHARTER ORDINANCE EXEMPTING THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS, FROM THE PROVISIONS OF K.S.A. 41-712 PROHIBITING ALCOHOLIC LIQUOR SALE ON SUNDAY AND CERTAIN HOLIDAYS, AND PROVIDING SUBSTITUTE AND ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS ON THE SAME SUBJECT ALLOWING ALCOHOLIC LIQUOR SALES ON SUNDAY AND CERTAIN HOLIDAYS.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. That the Governing Body of the City of Overland Park, Kansas, by the power vested in it by Article 12, Section 5, of the Constitution of the State of Kansas hereby elects and exempts itself from and makes inapplicable to it, the provisions of K.S.A. 41-712, which applies to this city but is part of an enactment commonly known as the Kansas Liquor Control Act, as enacted in Chapter 242 of the Session Laws of 1949, which enactment applies to this city but does not apply uniformly to all cities relating to the sale of alcoholic liquor on Sunday and certain holidays and hereby provides substitute and additional provisions as hereinafter set forth.

SECTION 2. The following is hereby substituted for the provisions of K.S.A. 41-712, as amended:

Sale at retail; forbidden on certain days; hours of sale, exception. No person shall sell at retail any alcoholic liquor: (1) on Thanksgiving Day or Christmas Day; or (2) before 9:00 a.m. or after 11:00 p.m. Monday through Saturday; or (3) before 11:00 a.m. or after 8:00 p.m. on Sunday.

SECTION 3. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two (2) consecutive weeks in The Overland Park Sun, an official City newspaper.

SECTION 4. THIS IS A CHARTER ORDINANCE AND SHALL TAKE EFFECT 61 DAYS AFTER FINAL PUBLICATION UNLESS WITHIN 60 DAYS OF ITS FINAL PUBLICATION A PETITION SIGNED BY A NUMBER OF ELECTORS OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK EQUAL TO NOT LESS THAN TEN PERCENT OF THE NUMBER OF ELECTORS WHO VOTED AT THE LAST PRECEDING REGULAR CITY ELECTION SHALL BE FILED IN THE OFFICE OF THE CITY CLERK OF OVERLAND PARK DEMANDING AN ELECTION ON THE CHARTER ORDINANCE IN WHICH CASE THE CHARTER ORDINANCE SHALL BECOME EFFECTIVE ONLY IF AND WHEN APPROVED BY A MAJORITY OF THE ELECTORS VOTING THEREON.

PASSED BY THE GOVERNING BODY NOT LESS THAN TWO-THIRDS OF THE MEMBERS ELECT VOTING IN FAVOR THEREOF THE 19th DAY OF MAY, 2003.

Ed Eilert, Mayor

Marian Cook

City Clerk
