Charter Ordinance No. Eighty-Three

(Note: All or parts of this charter ordinance have been repealed. See notes at the end of the ordinance.)

A CHARTER ORDINANCE EXEMPTING THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS,

FROM SECTIONS 13-14a01, 13-14a02, 13-14a03, 13-14a04, 13-14a05, 13-14a06, 13-14a07, 13-14a08, 13-14a09, 13-14a11, 13-14a12, AND 13-14a13 OF THE KANSAS STATUTES ANNOTATED PERTAINING TO FIRE AND POLICE DEPARTMENT RETIREMENT SYSTEMS, AND PROVIDING SUBSTITUTE AND ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS ON THE SAME SUBJECT AND REPEALING OVERLAND PARK CHARTER ORDINANCE NUMBER TWENTY- SEVEN.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. The City of Overland Park, Kansas, by the power vested in it by Article 12, Section 5 of the Constitution of the state of Kansas, hereby elects to exempt itself from and make inapplicable to it Sections 13-14a0l, 13-14a02, 13-14a03, 13-14a04, 13-14a05, 13-14a06, 13-14a07, 13-14a08, 13-14a09, 13-14a11, 13-14a12, and 13-14a13 of the Kansas Statutes Annotated and provide substitute and additional provisions therefor; Said sections relate to Fire and Police Department Retirement Systems, an enactment of the legislature which is applicable to the City of Overland Park, but not uniformly applicable to all cities.

SECTION 2. The Overland Park, Kansas, Police Department Retirement Plan (the “Police Department Plan”) and the Overland Park, Kansas, Police Department Retirement Trust Agreement (the “Police Department Trust Agreement”), as previously established under authority of Overland Park Charter Ordinance Number Eight and Overland Park Ordinances PEN-497, PEN-497A, PEN-497B and PEN-842 and reestablished by Charter Ordinance Number Twenty Seven, are hereby further reestablished and reaffirmed.

The Overland Park, Kansas, Fire Department Retirement Plan, as originally established by the Overland Park Fire Department, Incorporated, as of January 1, 1969, subsequently amended and restated by the Overland Park Fire Department, Incorporated, and then assumed by the City of Overland Park, Kansas, as of September 1, 2003 (the “Fire Department Plan”), and the Overland Park, Kansas, Fire Department Retirement Trust, as established by the Overland Park Fire Department, Incorporated, and then assumed, amended, and restated by the City of Overland Park, Kansas, as of September 1, 2003 (the “Fire Department Trust Agreement”), are hereby reestablished and reaffirmed.

The Governing Body is authorized to amend, by resolution, either the Police Department Plan or Trust Agreement or the Fire Department Plan or Trust Agreement, provided that such amendments shall not conflict with any provisions of this Charter Ordinance.

SECTION 3.

  1. A.
    Police Department Trust Agreement. There shall be five trustees to act as provided in the Police Department Trust Agreement. The Mayor shall appoint three trustees, although the appointment of two trustees shall be with the consent of the City Council. The three trustees to be appointed by the Mayor shall be residents of the City of Overland Park, Kansas. Beginning in 1982, such trustees shall be appointed for designated terms of three years, two years, and one year and until their successors are appointed and qualified. In years after 1982, each appointed trustee shall serve three years and until his or her successor is appointed and qualified.

    Two additional trustees shall be elected from among the participating membership of the Police Department Retirement Plan, by a majority vote of the members of the Plan casting ballots. In 1982, trustees elected by the participating members shall serve for specified terms of two years and three years and until their successors are appointed and qualified. At each future election, a trustee elected by the participating members shall serve for a period of three years and until his or her successor is appointed and qualified.

    The term of each trustee of the Police Department Trust Agreement shall commence on the second Tuesday of April following his or her appointment or election. Other provisions concerning the appointment, election, resignation, or removal of a trustee may be specified in the Police Department Trust Agreement, so long as such provisions do not conflict with any provisions of this Charter Ordinance.

  2. B.
    Fire Department Trust Agreement. There shall be five trustees to act as provided in the Fire Department Trust Agreement. The Mayor shall appoint three trustees, although the appointment of two such trustees shall be with the consent of the City Council. The three trustees to be appointed by the Mayor shall be residents of the City of Overland Park, Kansas. Effective as of September 1, 2003, such trustees shall be appointed for designated terms of three years, two years, and one year and until their successors are appointed and qualified. In years after 2003, each appointed trustee shall serve three years and until his or her successor is appointed and qualified.

    Two additional trustees shall be elected from among the participating membership of the Fire Department Retirement Plan, by a majority vote of the members of the Plan casting ballots. Such trustees in office as of September 1, 2003, shall serve remaining terms of two years and one year and until their successors are elected and qualified. In future years, a trustee elected by the participating members shall serve for a period of three years and until his or her successor is elected and qualified.

    The term of each trustee of the Fire Department Trust Agreement shall commence immediately upon such trustee’s acceptance of his or her appointment or election. Other provisions concerning the appointment, election, resignation, or removal of a trustee may be specified in the Fire Department Trust Agreement, so long as such provisions do not conflict with any provisions of this Charter Ordinance.

SECTION 4. One or more policies providing disability income and life insurance for the benefit of police officers, fire fighters and their dependents shall be purchased and maintained to supplement the provisions of the Police Department Plan, the Fire Department Plan, and their related Trust Agreements.

SECTION 5. For the purpose of funding the Plans and purchasing any insurance described in Section 4 of this Charter Ordinance, the City shall make annual contributions from the General Fund. Said contributions shall be made in compliance with K.S.A. 2002 Supp. 12-5002 or any amendments thereto, and shall be maintained in order to fund the Plans on an actuarially-sound basis.

In addition thereto, funds shall be utilized for the Plans and said insurance which are obtained by gifts, grants, bequests, gratuities and proceeds from lost or stolen securities, money or personal property which shall remain unclaimed in the possession of the City for six months, together with the proceeds of all unclaimed or confiscated property of any nature which shall have been in the possession of the City for a period of six months, and the City is hereby authorized to sell at public auction any such lost, stolen or unclaimed property.

SECTION 6. Payments upon retirement, discharge, disability, or death to any member or beneficiary of the Police Department Plan or the Fire Department Plan shall be established and paid out in accordance with the terms of the Plans and the Trust Agreements.

SECTION 7. Existing Overland Park Charter Ordinance Number Twenty-Seven is hereby repealed.

SECTION 8. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in the Overland Park Sun, an official City newspaper.

SECTION 9. This is a Charter Ordinance and shall take effect sixty-one (61) days after final publication unless within 60 days of its final publication a petition signed by a number of electors of the city of overland park equal to not less than ten percent of the number of electors who voted at the last preceding regular city election shall be filed in the office of the city clerk of overland park demanding an election on the charter ordinance in which case the charter ordinance shall become effective only if and when approved by a majority of the electors voting thereon.

PASSED by the Governing Body not less than two-thirds of the members-elect voting in favor thereof the 11th day of August, 2003.

Ed Eilert, Mayor

ATTEST:

Marian Cook

City Clerk

APPROVED AS TO FORM:

Michael R. Santos

Senior Assistant City Attorney

Passed: 8/11/03

Published: 8/14/03

8/21/03

Effective: 10/21/03

Entire ordinance repealed by Charter Ordinance No. Ninety-Two

Effective on: 1/1/1901

Charter Ordinance No. Eighty-Four (repealed by Ch. Ord. No. NINETY-SEVEN EFFECTIVE 12/31/2016)

(Note: All or parts of this charter ordinance have been repealed effective December 31, 2016. See notes at the end of the ordinance.)

A CHARTER ORDINANCE RELATING TO THE FORM OF GOVERNMENT OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS; EXEMPTING THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS, FROM K.S.A. 12-1009 THROUGH 12-1015, 12-1036a. THROUGH 12-1036h., 25-2107, 25-2108a., 25-2109, AND 25-2113; REPEALING CHARTER ORDINANCE NUMBERS THIRTY-FOUR, FORTY, FORTY-THREE, SEVENTY-THREE, SEVENTY-SIX, SEVENTY-SEVEN, SEVENTY-EIGHT AND EIGHTY-ONE AND PROVIDING SUBSTITUTE AND ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS ESTABLISHING A MAYOR-COUNCIL-MANAGER FORM OF GOVERNMENT FOR THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

Article I. Statutes made inapplicable.

The City of Overland Park, Kansas, by the power vested in it by Article 12, Section 5, of the Constitution of the State of Kansas, hereby elects to exempt itself from and make inapplicable to it the provisions of K.S.A. 12-1009 through 12-1015, 12-1036a. through 12-1036h., 25-2107, 25-2108a., 25-2109, and 25-2113, and provide substitute and additional provisions as hereinafter set forth in this charter ordinance. Such referenced provisions are either enactments or a part thereof which are applicable to this city but are not applicable uniformly to all cities.

Article II. City council, mayor and council president.

Section 2.1. Composition and eligibility.

  1. A.
    Composition. The governing body shall consist of a mayor elected by the city as a whole and twelve (12) council members elected by wards. Each ward shall be represented by two council members.
  2. B.
    Eligibility. The mayor and each council member shall be a citizen of the United States and a qualified elector of Overland Park, Kansas, and must reside in the city at the time of filing for election and thereafter for the duration of their terms. Council members from wards shall be residents of their respective wards at the time of filing for election and thereafter for the duration of their terms unless removal from the ward is the result of reapportionment.

Section 2.2. Elections and terms.

  1. A.
    Nonpartisan elections. City elections shall be nonpartisan. Laws applicable to elections occurring at the same time as city elections shall apply to city elections to the extent that the same are not in conflict with the provisions of Article 21 of Chapter 25 of the Kansas Statutes Annotated.
  2. B.
    Date of primary and general city elections. The primary election of the city officers shall be held on the Tuesday preceding by five weeks the first Tuesday in April of every odd-numbered year. The general election of the city officers shall be held on the first Tuesday in April of every odd-numbered year. Both elections shall be conducted in the manner prescribed in this charter ordinance and applicable state law.
  3. C.
    Filing deadlines for city elections. The filing deadline for all city elections shall be twelve noon of the Tuesday preceding by ten weeks the first Tuesday of April of each election year.
  4. D.
    Procedure for conduct of primary city elections.
    1. 1.
      In 2005 and in each subsequent year in which the term of the mayor expires, if no more than two (2) candidates have filed for the office of mayor, both such candidates shall automatically be listed on the general city election ballot; but if more than two (2) candidates have filed for the office of mayor, a primary city election at large shall be held for the nomination of two (2) candidates for the office. Each qualified elector of the city shall be entitled to vote for one (1) candidate. The two (2) candidates receiving the highest number of votes cast shall be placed on the general city election ballot as the candidates nominated for election as mayor.
    2. 2.
      In 2005 and in each subsequent year in which the term of a council member expires, if no more than two (2) candidates have filed for the office of council member to be filled, both such candidates shall automatically be listed on the general city election ballot; but if more than two (2) candidates have filed for the office of council member to be filled, a primary city election shall be held within each such ward for the nomination of two (2) candidates from that ward. Each qualified elector who is a resident of the ward shall be entitled to vote for one (1) candidate from his or her ward. The two (2) candidates from each such ward receiving the highest number of votes cast shall be placed on the general city election ballot as the candidates nominated for election from the ward.
  5. E.
    Procedure for conduct of general city elections.
    1. 1.
      In the general city election in 2005 and in each subsequent year in which the term of the mayor expires, all qualified electors of the city shall be entitled to vote on the election of the mayor. Of the candidate or candidates nominated or automatically listed for the office of mayor, the candidate receiving the highest number of votes in the general city election shall be elected as mayor.
    2. 2.
      In the general city election in 2005 and in each subsequent year in which the term of a council member expires, all qualified electors of the ward shall be entitled to vote on the election of the council member from that ward. Of the two (2) candidates nominated or automatically listed from each ward, the candidate receiving the highest number of votes in the general city election shall be elected as council member from the ward.
  6. F.
    Terms of office.
    1. 1.
      The incumbent members of the governing body shall continue in office for the duration of their terms as heretofore established.
    2. 2.
      At the primary and general city elections held in 2005 the position of mayor shall be filled by election, and the person so elected in 2005 shall succeed to the position of mayor. The person elected as mayor in 2005 and in subsequent elections shall hold the position of mayor for a term of four (4) years and until the person’s successor has been duly elected and qualified.
    3. 3.
      At the primary and general city elections held in 2005 all council member positions which were last filled by election in 2001 and the Ward Six council member position filled for an initial two-year term in 2003, shall be filled by election, and those persons so elected in 2005 shall succeed to those council member positions. Those persons elected as council members in 2005, and in subsequent elections, shall hold their positions for a term of four (4) years and until their successors are duly elected and qualified.
    4. 4.
      At the primary and general city elections held in 2007 all council member positions which were last filled by election in 2003 except for the Ward Six council member position filled for an initial two-year term in 2003 shall be filled by election. Those persons elected as council members in 2007, and in subsequent elections, shall hold their positions for a term of four (4) years and until their successors are duly elected and qualified.

*Section 2.3. Wards.

  1. A.
    Number of wards. The city shall be divided into six wards numbered one through six for purposes of the city elections held in 2003 and thereafter.
  2. B.
    Ward boundaries. Dividing lines between wards shall follow precinct boundaries established by the Johnson County Election Commissioner. The governing body of the city shall, by ordinance, adjust the ward boundaries to reflect approximately equal population effective in December of 2004. The governing body of the city may likewise adjust ward boundaries effective in December every two years thereafter but shall adjust the boundaries effective in December every four years thereafter. The governing body shall certify the boundaries so adjusted to the Johnson County Election Commissioner.

Section 2.4. Council president, election of, powers and duties.

  1. A.
    On the second Monday after the first Tuesday in the month of April of each year, the council shall elect, by seven (7) affirmative votes cast by signed written ballot, one of its members as president of the council who, in the absence or disability of the mayor shall become acting mayor. Abstentions and blanks shall not be counted as votes cast. Such council member shall retain all his voting rights and other prerogatives as council member while acting as mayor.
  2. B.
    Whenever the council president succeeds to the office of mayor pursuant to Section 2.5 B, below, the remaining membership of the council shall elect a new council president in the manner set out in Section 2.4 A, above.

**Section 2.5. Filling vacancies in office of mayor and council member.

  1. A.
    The mayor and council members shall remain in office until their successors have been duly elected and qualified. When there is a vacancy in the position of mayor, the president of the council, upon being qualified, shall become mayor. If such vacancy occurs before January 1 of an odd-numbered year leaving an unexpired term of more than two (2) years from said January 1, such successor shall serve in such position until a special election is held at the same time as the next regular city primary and general election. Such special election shall be held for the purpose of electing a replacement mayor to serve in such position for the remainder of the unexpired term. Such special election shall be held in the same manner as if the term were expiring and the term of the successor shall end upon election of a mayor for the unexpired term. In all other cases, the successor shall serve the remainder of the unexpired term.
  2. B.
    When the president of the council becomes mayor, there shall be a vacancy in the position of council president, which shall be filled by the remaining members of the council pursuant to Section 2.4, above, no later than the first regular council meeting following the date of occurrence of the vacancy. When the president of the council becomes mayor, there also shall be a vacancy in such council member's position on the council. Whenever a vacancy occurs for any reason in the position of council member, a screening committee composed of the mayor, president of the council and chair of the Finance, Administration and Economic Development Committee of the council shall recommend to the remaining members of the committee of the whole of the council and the mayor not to exceed two (2) persons as potential replacements within thirty (30) days from the date the vacancy is declared to have occurred by the members of the council remaining after the vacancy. The remaining members of the council and the mayor shall so declare a vacancy to have occurred at the first regular council meeting following the date of occurrence of the vacancy. Upon receipt of such recommendation from the screening committee, the committee of the whole of the council, by majority vote of the remaining members thereof and the mayor, may recommend to the remaining members of the council and the mayor either recommended replacement or may reject both such recommended replacements. Should the remaining members of the committee of the whole of the council and the mayor reject both such recommended replacements, the screening committee shall make a second recommendation of not to exceed two (2) persons other than the two persons originally recommended, as potential replacements for consideration by the remaining members of the committee of the whole of the council and the mayor within fifteen (15) days of such rejection. Upon receipt of the second recommendation from the screening committee, the committee of the whole of the council, by majority vote of the remaining members thereof and the mayor, may recommend to the remaining members of the council and the mayor either such recommended replacement or may reject both such recommended replacements. Should the remaining members of the committee of the whole of the council and the mayor reject both such recommended replacements, the council, by majority vote of the remaining members thereof and the mayor, shall appoint an elector qualified to fill the vacant position.
  3. C.
    If such vacancy occurs in the position of council member before January 1 of an odd-numbered year leaving an unexpired term of more than two (2) years from said January 1, such appointed replacement shall serve in such position until a special election is held at the same time as the next regular city primary and general election. Such special election shall be held for the purpose of electing a replacement to serve in such position for the remainder of the unexpired term. Such special election shall be held in the same manner as if the term were expiring and the term of the appointed member, if any, shall end upon election of a member for the unexpired term. In all other cases, the appointed replacement shall serve the remainder of the unexpired term.

Section 2.6. General powers and duties of the governing body; voting.

  1. A.
    General powers and duties. All powers of the city shall be vested in the governing body, except as the Constitution and Statutes of Kansas or this or other charter ordinances may provide otherwise. The governing body shall provide for the performance of all duties and obligations imposed on the city by law.
  2. B.
    Voting.
    1. 1.
      Any ordinance shall be passed by an affirmative vote of seven (7) council members unless a greater number of votes are specifically required by another provision of law; provided, that where the number of favorable votes is one less than required, the mayor shall have the power to cast the deciding vote in favor of the ordinance.
    2. 2.
      Any resolution, motion or any other action of the governing body except charter ordinances, upon which the mayor's vote shall be counted on every occasion, and except as otherwise provided herein or by law, shall be passed at a meeting at which a quorum is present if more than half of the votes cast by council members are affirmative votes; provided, that abstentions shall not be counted as votes cast and where the number of affirmative votes is one less than required, the mayor shall have the power to cast the deciding vote in favor of the resolution, motion or other action.

Section 2.7. Powers and duties of the mayor.

The mayor shall be the titular head of the city and shall preside at all meetings of the council. The mayor shall sign all ordinances and resolutions passed by the council; provided, that the mayor shall have the power to veto any ordinance or resolution passed by the council, except the mayor shall have no power to veto a charter ordinance, or appointments by the council for vacancies on the council. Any ordinance or resolution vetoed by the mayor may be passed over the veto by the vote of two-thirds of the whole number of the council members-elect, notwithstanding the veto; and if the mayor has failed to sign or veto such ordinance or resolution by the first regular meeting following the adoption of such ordinance or resolution, the same shall take effect without the mayor’s signature. It shall be the duty of the mayor to sign all contracts and agreements authorized by the governing body, except those contracts and agreements designated by the governing body to be entered into and signed by the city manager or his/her designee. The mayor shall represent the city governing body at all official city functions and shall be the official representative of the governing body at all meetings, conferences and negotiations relating to policy matters involving other governmental units and shall recommend council action relating thereto. The mayor shall be the official representative of the governing body at all conferences and meetings with citizens' groups and organizations interested in promoting the welfare and development of the city and shall recommend council action relative thereto. The mayor may annually communicate to the council in writing such information as he or she may possess with reference to the condition of the city, and recommend such measures as he or she may deem necessary and expedient. The mayor may inform the council concerning questions of policy and may make policy recommendations to the council.

Article III. City manager.

Section 3.1. In general.

The governing body and the city manager shall be governed by the provisions of K.S.A. 12-1009 to 12-1013, both sections inclusive, and K.S.A. 12-1015, and any amendments thereto, in the organization and administration of the city.

Section 3.2. Powers and duties of the city manager.

The city manager shall be responsible for the administration of all of the affairs of the city. The city manager shall see that the laws and ordinances of the city are enforced.

The city manager shall have the authority to appoint, promote, transfer, assign and demote, discipline, lay off, suspend, discharge, or remove all heads of departments, and all subordinate officers and employees of the city. All such appointments shall be made upon merit and fitness alone. Provided that, the city manager may delegate at his or her discretion such authority to appoint, promote, transfer, assign and demote, discipline, lay off, suspend, discharge, or remove subordinate officers and employees of the city to department directors and division heads. In addition, the city manager may, without notice, cause the affairs of any department or the conduct of any officer or employee to be examined. The city manager shall prepare and submit the annual budget to the governing body and also keep the city fully advised as to the financial conditions and needs of the city. The city manager may make recommendations to the governing body on all matters concerning the welfare of the city and shall have a seat, but no vote, in all of the public meetings of the governing body. The city manager, or his/her designee, may enter into, execute, approve and accept, on behalf of the city, all contracts, agreements and other documents as designated by the governing body.

Article IV. Civil service commission.

Notwithstanding the provisions of Article III, above, the city shall have a civil service commission and such civil service commission shall have the jurisdiction, powers, duties and responsibilities as set forth in the ordinary ordinances of the city.

Article V. Repeal of charter ordinances.

That Charter Ordinance Nos. Thirty-Four, Forty, Forty-Three, Seventy-Three, Seventy-Six, Seventy-Seven, Seventy-Eight and Eighty-One be and the same are hereby repealed.

Article VI. Severability.

It is hereby declared to be the intention of the governing body that the sections, subsections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this charter ordinance are severable, and if any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph, subsection or section of this charter ordinance shall be declared unconstitutional or otherwise invalid by the valid judgment or decree of any court of competent jurisdiction, such unconstitutionality or invalidity shall not affect any of the remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs, subsections and sections of this charter ordinance, since the same would have been enacted by the governing body without the incorporation into this charter ordinance of any unconstitutional or otherwise invalid phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph, subsection or section.

Article VII. Publication.

This charter ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in The Overland Park Sun, an official City newspaper.

Article VIII. Effectiveness.

THIS IS A CHARTER ORDINANCE AND SHALL TAKE EFFECT 61 DAYS AFTER FINAL PUBLICATION UNLESS WITHIN 60 DAYS OF ITS FINAL PUBLICATION A PETITION SIGNED BY A NUMBER OF ELECTORS OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK EQUAL TO NOT LESS THAN TEN PERCENT OF THE NUMBER OF ELECTORS WHO VOTED AT THE LAST PRECEDING REGULAR CITY ELECTION SHALL BE FILED IN THE OFFICE OF THE CITY CLERK OF OVERLAND PARK DEMANDING AN ELECTION ON THE CHARTER ORDINANCE IN WHICH CASE THE CHARTER ORDINANCE SHALL BECOME EFFECTIVE ONLY IF AND WHEN APPROVED BY A MAJORITY OF THE ELECTORS VOTING THEREON.

PASSED by the Governing Body not less than two-thirds of the members elect voting in favor thereof the 3rd day of November, 2003.

Ed Eilert, Mayor

ATTEST:

Marian Cook

City Clerk

APPROVED AS TO FORM:

Robert J. Watson

City Attorney

Charter Ordinance No. Eighty-Five

A CHARTER ORDINANCE EXEMPTING THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS, FROM THE PROVISIONS OF K.S.A. 12-4209, AS AMENDED BY SECTION 3 OF CHAPTER 71, 2004 SESSION LAWS OF KANSAS; K.S.A. 12-4213, AS AMENDED BY SECTION 4 OF CHAPTER 71, 2004 SESSION LAWS OF KANSAS; K.S.A. 12-4301, K.S.A. 12-4501, PERTAINING TO THE KANSAS CODE OF PROCEDURE FOR MUNICIPAL COURTS.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. That the Governing Body of the City of Overland Park, Kansas, by the power vested in it by Article 12, Section 5 of the Constitution of the State of Kansas hereby elects to exempt itself and make inapplicable to it the provisions of K.S.A. 12-4209, as amended by Section 3 of Chapter 71, 2004 Session Laws of Kansas; K.S.A. 12-4213, as amended by Section 4 of Chapter 71, 2004 Session Laws of Kansas; K.S.A. 12-4301, K.S.A. 12-4501; and hereby provides substitute and additional provisions as hereinafter set forth in Section 2 of this Charter Ordinance. Such referenced provisions are part of the Kansas Code of Procedure for Municipal Courts, an enactment of the legislature which is applicable to the City of Overland Park, but not uniformly applicable to all cities.

SECTION 2. Substitute and additional provisions on the subjects addressed by K.S.A. 12-4209, as amended by Section 3 of Chapter 71, 2004 Session Laws of Kansas; K.S.A. 12-4213, as amended by Section 4 of Chapter 71, 2004 Session Laws of Kansas; K.S.A. 12-4301 and K.S.A. 12-4501 will be contained in an ordinary ordinance.

SECTION 3. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in The Overland Park Sun, an official City newspaper.

SECTION 4. This is a Charter Ordinance and shall take effect sixty-one (61) days after final publication unless within 60 days of its final publication a petition signed by a number of electors of the city of overland park equal to not less than ten percent of the number of electors who voted at the last preceding regular city election shall be filed in the office of the city clerk of overland park demanding an election on the charter ordinance in which case the charter ordinance shall become effective only if and when approved by a majority of the electors voting thereon.

PASSED by the Governing Body not less than two-thirds of the members-elect voting in favor thereof the 12th day of July, 2004.

Ed Eilert, Mayor

    Ward Boundaries. Dividing lines between wards shall follow precinct boundaries established by the Johnson County Election Commissioner. PASSED by the Governing Body not less than two-thirds of the members elect voting in favor thereof the 11th day of October, 2004.

Ed Eilert, Mayor

Charter Ordinance No. Eighty-Seven (Repealed by Chapter 205 of the 2005 Session Laws of Kansas)

(Note: All or parts of this charter ordinance have been repealed. See notes at the end of the ordinance.)

A CHARTER ORDINANCE AMENDING AND REPEALING EXISTING SECTION 2 OF CHARTER ORDINANCE NO. SIXTY-FOUR RELATING TO PROHIBITION OF CERTAIN EXCISE TAXES; AND PROVIDING A SUBSTITUTE AND ADDITIONAL PROVISION AUTHORIZING THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS, TO LEVY AN EXCISE TAX ON THE ACT OF PLATTING REAL PROPERTY IN THE CITY, TO GRANT CREDITS AGAINST EXCISE TAXES PAID ON THE ACT OF PLATTING REAL PROPERTY IN THE CITY FOR CONTRIBUTIONS PREVIOUSLY MADE FOR THOROUGHFARE IMPROVEMENTS, TO GRANT EXEMPTIONS, TO LEVY AN EXCISE TAX ON ADMISSIONS TO EVENTS TO BE HELD IN AN ARENA TO BE CONSTRUCTED AND OWNED BY THE CITY ON LAND TO BE OWNED BY THE CITY ON THE NORTH SIDE OF 115TH STREET EAST OF METCALF AVENUE, AND TO LEVY AN EXCISE TAX ON CONCESSION SERVICES AND FOOD AND BEVERAGE SERVICES SOLD IN AN ARENA TO BE CONSTRUCTED AND OWNED BY THE CITY ON LAND TO BE OWNED BY THE CITY ON THE NORTH SIDE OF 115TH STREET EAST OF METCALF AVENUE.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. Section 2 of Charter Ordinance No. Sixty-Four of the City of Overland Park be and the same is hereby amended to read as follows:

No city or county shall levy or impose an excise tax or a tax in the nature of an excise, other than a retailers’ sales tax and a compensating use tax, upon the sale or transfer of personal or real property, or the use thereof, or the rendering of a service, but the provisions of this section shall not be construed as prohibiting any city from (a) contracting with a utility for a fixed charge based upon a percentage of gross receipts derived from the service permitted by grant, right, privilege or franchise to such utility; (b) imposing an occupational tax or license fee for the privilege of engaging in any business, trade, occupation or profession, or rendering or furnishing any service, but the determination of any such license fee shall not be based upon any amount the licensee has received from the sale or transfer of personal or real property, or for the rendering or furnishing of a service, or on the income of the licensee; (c) levying any occupation tax or license fee imposed by such city prior to the effective date of this act; (d) levying an excise tax on the act of platting real property in the city, granting credits against excise taxes paid on the act of platting real property in the city for contributions previously made for thoroughfare improvements, and granting exemptions; (e) levying an excise tax on admissions to events to be held in an arena to be constructed and owned by the city on land to be owned by the city on the north side of 115th Street east of Metcalf Avenue; or (f) levying an excise tax on

concession services and food and beverage services sold within an arena to be constructed and owned by the city on land to be owned by the city on the north side of 115th Street east of Metcalf Avenue. No license fee described in subsection (b) of this section shall be imposed upon any utility contracting with and subject to a charge, described in subsection (a) of this section, by such city.

SECTION 2. Existing Section 2 of Charter Ordinance No. Sixty-Four is hereby repealed.

SECTION 3. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in The Overland Park Sun, an official City newspaper.

SECTION 4. THIS IS A CHARTER ORDINANCE AND SHALL TAKE EFFECT 61 DAYS AFTER FINAL PUBLICATION UNLESS WITHIN 60 DAYS OF ITS FINAL PUBLICATION A PETITION SIGNED BY A NUMBER OF ELECTORS OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK EQUAL TO NOT LESS THAN TEN PERCENT OF THE NUMBER OF ELECTORS WHO VOTED AT THE LAST PRECEDING REGULAR CITY ELECTION SHALL BE FILED IN THE OFFICE OF THE CITY CLERK OF OVERLAND PARK DEMANDING AN ELECTION ON THE CHARTER ORDINANCE, IN WHICH CASE THE CHARTER ORDINANCE SHALL BECOME EFFECTIVE ONLY IF AND WHEN APPROVED BY A MAJORITY OF THE ELECTORS VOTING THEREON.

PASSED by the Governing Body not less than two-thirds of the members elect voting in favor thereof the 18th day of October, 2004.

Ed Eilert, Mayor

ATTEST:

Marian Cook

City Clerk

APPROVED AS TO FORM:

Robert J. Watson

City Attorney

Charter Ordinance No. Eighty-Eight

(Note: All or parts of this charter ordinance have been repealed. See notes at the end of the ordinance.)

A CHARTER ORDINANCE EXEMPTING THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS, FROM SECTION 12-1036e OF THE KANSAS STATUTES ANNOTATED; PROVIDING SUBSTITUTE PROVISIONS ON THE SALARIES OF THE MAYOR AND CITY COUNCIL MEMBERS; PROVIDING FOR MAYOR AND COUNCIL MEMBER BENEFITS AND ALLOWANCES; AND REPEALING CHARTER ORDINANCE NO. EIGHTY.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. The City of Overland Park, Kansas, a Mayor-Council-City Manager city of the first class, by the power vested in it by Article 12, Section 5 of the Constitution of the State of Kansas, hereby elects to and does exempt itself from and make inapplicable to it, Section 12-1036e of the Kansas Statutes Annotated, which law applies only to Mayor-Council-City Manager cities of the first class, and to provide substitute provisions as herein provided.

SECTION 2. Commencing January 1, 2002, the salary of each City Council Member shall be an amount not to exceed $12,000 per year, payable monthly.

SECTION 3. Commencing January 1, 2002, the City Council Member who serves as President of the Council also shall receive an additional amount not to exceed $2,400 per year, payable monthly while serving in that capacity.

SECTION 4. Commencing January 1, 2002, the salary of the Mayor shall be an amount not to exceed $24,000 per year, payable monthly.

SECTION 5. The procedure for determining the amount of the salary of each Council Member, the President of the Council and the Mayor, up to the not to exceed amount, shall be determined by resolution of the governing body.

SECTION 6. The not to exceed amount of the salaries of the Council Members, President of the Council and Mayor shall be reviewed at least once every four years.

SECTION 7. Effective May 1, 2006, the Mayor shall continue to receive a monthly “automobile expense allowance” of $400 and the Council President and Council members shall receive a monthly “automobile expense allowance” of $100. In addition to the monthly “automobile expense allowance,” the Mayor, Council President and Council members may also receive a mileage reimbursement for “long distance trips.” Reimbursement for such “long distance trips” shall be subject to the same standards, limitations and rates applicable to the reimbursement of

City employees who receive automobile allowances for comparable long distance mileage expenses at the time the Mayor, Council President or Council members submit a request for reimbursement for such “long distance trips.”

SECTION 8. Members of the Governing Body may choose not to receive the monthly “automobile expense allowance.” Members of the Governing Body who choose not to receive the “automobile expense allowance” shall continue to be eligible to submit documented receipts and statements for mileage reimbursement in accordance with existing City policy.

SECTION 9. Future changes in the amount of the “automobile expense allowance” shall be established by ordinary ordinance.

SECTION 10. Governing Body benefits not related to salary or expense allowances shall be established by resolution of the Governing Body.

SECTION 11. Charter Ordinance No. Eighty is hereby repealed.

SECTION 12. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in The Overland Park Sun, an official City newspaper.

SECTION 13. THIS IS A CHARTER ORDINANCE AND SHALL TAKE EFFECT 61 DAYS AFTER FINAL PUBLICATION UNLESS WITHIN 60 DAYS OF ITS FINAL, PUBLICATION A PETITION-SIGNED BY A NUMBER OF ELECTORS OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK EQUAL TO NOT LESS THAN TEN PERCENT OF THE NUMBER OF ELECTORS WHO VOTED AT THE LAST PRECEDING REGULAR CITY ELECTION SHALL BE FILED IN THE OFFICE OF THE CITY CLERK OF OVERLAND PARK DEMANDING AN ELECTION ON THE CHARTER ORDINANCE IN WHICH CASE THE CHARTER ORDINANCE SHALL BECOME EFFECTIVE ONLY IF AND WHEN APPROVED BY A MAJORITY OF THE ELECTORS VOTING THEREON.

PASSED by the Governing Body not less than two-thirds of the members elect voting in favor thereof the 6th day of February, 2006.

(s) Carl Gerlach

ATTEST: Carl Gerlach, Mayor

(s) Marian Cook

Marian Cook City Clerk

APPROVED AS TO FORM: Passed: 2/6/06

(s) Michael R. Santos Published: 2/9/06 and 2/16/06

Michael R. Santos 61 days: 4/18/06

Senior Assistant City Attorney Effective: 4/18/06

Entire ordinance repealed by Charter Ordinance No. Ninety-Six

Charter Ordinance No. Eighty-Nine (REPEALED BY CH. ORD. NO. NINETY-SEVEN EFFECTIVE 12/31/2016)

(Note: All or parts of this charter ordinance have been repealed effective December 31, 2016. See notes at the end of the ordinance.)

A CHARTER ORDINANCE AMENDING AND REPEALING EXISTING SECTION 2.5 OF CHARTER ORDINANCE NO. EIGHTY-FOUR RELATING TO FILLING VACANCIES IN OFFICE OF MAYOR AND COUNCIL MEMBER.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. Section 2.5 of Charter Ordinance No. Eighty-Four of the City of Overland Park be and the same is hereby amended to read as follows:

Section 2.5. Filling vacancies in office of mayor and council member.

  1. A.
    The mayor and council members shall remain in office until their successors have been duly elected and qualified. When there is a vacancy in the position of mayor, the president of the council shall serve as acting Mayor until the Council elects a member to serve as Mayor. The Council shall elect a member to serve as Mayor within thirty days of the vacancy in the office of Mayor. If such vacancy occurs before January 1 of an odd-numbered year leaving an unexpired term of more than two (2) years from said January 1, such successor shall serve in such position until a special election is held at the same time as the next regular city primary and general election. Such special election shall be held for the purpose of electing a replacement mayor to serve in such position for the remainder of the unexpired term. Such special election shall be held in the same manner as if the term were expiring and the term of the successor shall end upon election of a mayor for the unexpired term. In all other cases, the successor shall serve the remainder of the unexpired term. Any vacancy on the Council created by the Council’s election of a member to the position of Mayor would be filled in accordance with the provisions of this Section
  2. B.
    Should the president of the council be elected mayor in accordance with the provisions of this Section, there shall be a vacancy in the position of council president, which shall be filled by the remaining members of the council pursuant to Section 2.4, above, no later than the first regular council meeting following the date of occurrence of the vacancy. When the president of the council becomes mayor, there also shall be a vacancy in such council member's position on the council. Whenever a vacancy occurs for any reason in the position of council member, a screening committee composed of the mayor, president of the council and chair of the Finance, Administration and Economic Development Committee of the council shall recommend to the remaining members of the committee of the whole of the council and the mayor not to exceed two (2) persons as potential replacements within thirty (30) days from the date the vacancy is declared to have occurred by the members of the council remaining after the vacancy. The remaining members of the council and the mayor shall so declare a vacancy to have occurred at the first regular council meeting following the date of occurrence of the vacancy. Upon receipt of such recommendation from the screening committee, the committee of the whole of the council, by majority vote of the remaining members thereof and the mayor, may recommend to the remaining members of the council and the mayor either recommended replacement or may reject both such recommended replacements. Should the remaining members of the committee of the whole of the council and the mayor reject both such recommended replacements, the screening committee shall make a second recommendation of not to exceed two (2) persons other than the two persons originally recommended, as potential replacements for consideration by the remaining members of the committee of the whole of the council and the mayor within fifteen (15) days of such rejection. Upon receipt of the second recommendation from the screening committee, the committee of the whole of the council, by majority vote of the remaining members thereof and the mayor, may recommend to the remaining members of the council and the mayor either such recommended replacement or may reject both such recommended replacements. Should the remaining members of the committee of the whole of the council and the mayor reject both such recommended replacements, the council, by majority vote of the remaining members thereof and the mayor, shall appoint an elector qualified to fill the vacant position.
  3. C.
    If such vacancy occurs in the position of council member before January 1 of an odd-numbered year leaving an unexpired term of more than two (2) years from said January 1, such appointed replacement shall serve in such position until a special election is held at the same time as the next regular city primary and general election. Such special election shall be held for the purpose of electing a replacement to serve in such position for the remainder of the unexpired term. Such special election shall be held in the same manner as if the term were expiring and the term of the appointed member, if any, shall end upon election of a member for the unexpired term. In all other cases, the appointed replacement shall serve the remainder of the unexpired term.

SECTION 2. Existing Section 2.5 of Charter Ordinance No. Eighty-Four is hereby repealed.

SECTION 3. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in The Overland Park Sun, an official City newspaper.

SECTION 4. THIS IS A CHARTER ORDINANCE AND SHALL TAKE EFFECT 61 DAYS AFTER FINAL PUBLICATION UNLESS WITHIN 60 DAYS OF ITS FINAL PUBLICATION A PETITION SIGNED BY A NUMBER OF ELECTORS OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK EQUAL TO NOT LESS THAN TEN PERCENT OF THE NUMBER OF ELECTORS WHO VOTED AT THE LAST PRECEDING REGULAR CITY ELECTION SHALL BE FILED IN THE OFFICE OF THE CITY CLERK OF OVERLAND PARK DEMANDING AN ELECTION ON THE CHARTER ORDINANCE, IN WHICH CASE THE CHARTER ORDINANCE SHALL BECOME EFFECTIVE ONLY IF AND WHEN APPROVED BY A MAJORITY OF THE ELECTORS VOTING THEREON.

PASSED by the Governing Body not less than two-thirds of the members elect voting in favor thereof the 16th day of October, 2006.

(s) Carl Gerlach

Carl Gerlach, Mayor

ATTEST:

(s) Marian Cook

Marian Cook

City Clerk

APPROVED AS TO FORM:

(s) Robert J. Watson

Robert J. Watson

Charter Ordinance No. Ninety

A CHARTER ORDINANCE EXEMPTING THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK FROM K.S.A. 64-101 WHICH RELATES TO THE QUALIFICATIONS AND METHOD OF DESIGNATING OFFICIAL CITY NEWSPAPERS, AND PROVIDING SUBSTITUTE AND ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS ON THE SAME SUBJECT; AND REPEALING CHARTER ORDINANCE NO. FORTY-FIVE OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. The City of Overland Park, Kansas, by the power vested in it by Article 12, Section 5, of the constitution of the State of Kansas, hereby elects to exempt itself from and make inapplicable to it the provisions of K.S.A. 64-101 which relates to the qualifications and method of designating official City newspapers and hereby provides substitute and additional provisions as set forth herein. Such referenced statute, K.S.A. 64-101 is applicable to this City but is not uniformly applicable to all cities.

SECTION 2. No legal notices, advertisements, or publications of any kind required or provided by any of the laws of Kansas or the City of Overland Park, Kansas, to be published in a newspaper shall have any force or effect unless the same be published in an official City newspaper.

SECTION 3. The Governing Body shall designate by resolution a newspaper or newspapers as the official City newspaper or newspapers for publication of legal notices, advertisements, or publications of any kind which may be required or provided by the laws of the state of Kansas or the City of Overland Park, Kansas, to be published in a newspaper.

SECTION 4. Any newspaper designated as an official City newspaper must meet the following qualifications:

  1. a.
    It must be published at least weekly fifty (50) times a year and have been so published for at least five (5) years prior to publication of any official publication; and
  2. b.
    It shall have a general circulation on a daily or weekly basis in Johnson County, Kansas, and shall not be a trade, religious or fraternal publication; and
  3. c.
    It must be published in Johnson County, Kansas.

SECTION 5. Nothing in this Charter Ordinance shall invalidate the publication in any newspaper which at the time of publication had been designated as an official City newspaper.

SECTION 6. Charter Ordinance No. Forty-Five is hereby repealed.

SECTION 7. THIS IS A CHARTER ORDINANCE AND SHALL TAKE EFFECT 61 DAYS AFTER FINAL PUBLICATION UNLESS WITHIN 60 DAYS OF ITS FINAL, PUBLICATION A PETITION-SIGNED BY A NUMBER OF ELECTORS OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK EQUAL TO NOT LESS THAN TEN PERCENT OF THE NUMBER OF ELECTORS WHO VOTED AT THE LAST PRECEDING REGULAR CITY ELECTION SHALL BE FILED IN THE OFFICE OF THE CITY CLERK OF OVERLAND PARK DEMANDING AN ELECTION ON THE CHARTER ORDINANCE IN WHICH CASE THE CHARTER ORDINANCE SHALL BECOME EFFECTIVE ONLY IF AND WHEN APPROVED BY A MAJORITY OF THE ELECTORS VOTING THEREON.

SECTION 8. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in The Olathe Daily News, an official City newspaper.

PASSED by the Governing Body of the City of Overland Park, Kansas, not less than two-thirds of the members elect voting in favor thereof, this 4th day of December, 2006.

(s) Carl Gerlach

Carl Gerlach, Mayor

ATTEST:

(s) Marian Cook

Marian Cook, City Clerk

(s) Michael R. Santos

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 2. The Governing Body of the City of Overland Park, Kansas, is hereby authorized to levy a transient guest tax at a rate not to exceed nine percent (9%) upon the gross receipts derived from or paid by transient guests for sleeping accommodations, exclusive of charges for incidental services or facilities, in any hotel, motel or tourist court. Charter Ordinance No. Ninety-Two (Repealed by Ch. Ord. No. 94)

(Note: All or parts of this charter ordinance have been repealed. See notes at the end of the ordinance.)

A CHARTER ORDINANCE EXEMPTING THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS,

FROM SECTIONS 13-14a01, 13-14a02, 13-14a03, 13-14a04, 13-14a05, 13-14a06, 13-14a07, 13-14a08, 13-14a09, 13-14a11, 13-14a12, AND 13-14a13 OF THE KANSAS STATUTES ANNOTATED PERTAINING TO FIRE AND POLICE DEPARTMENT RETIREMENT SYSTEMS, AND PROVIDING SUBSTITUTE AND ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS ON THE SAME SUBJECT AND REPEALING OVERLAND PARK CHARTER ORDINANCE NUMBER EIGHTY-THREE.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. The City of Overland Park, Kansas, by the power vested in it by Article 12, Section 5 of the Constitution of the state of Kansas, hereby elects to exempt itself from and make inapplicable to it Sections 13-14a0l, 13-14a02, 13-14a03, 13-14a04, 13-14a05, 13-14a06, 13-14a07, 13-14a08, 13-14a09, 13-14a11, 13-14a12, and 13-14a13 of the Kansas Statutes Annotated and provide substitute and additional provisions therefor; said sections relate to Fire and Police Department Retirement Systems, an enactment of the legislature which is applicable to the City of Overland Park, but not uniformly applicable to all cities.

SECTION 2. The Overland Park, Kansas, Police Department Retirement Plan (the “Police Department Plan”) and the Overland Park, Kansas, Police Department Retirement Trust Agreement (the “Police Department Trust Agreement”), as previously established under authority of Overland Park Charter Ordinance Number Eight and Overland Park Ordinances PEN-497, PEN-497A, PEN-497B and PEN-842 and reestablished by Charter Ordinance Number Twenty Seven and Charter Ordinance No. Eighty-Three are hereby further reestablished and reaffirmed.

The Overland Park, Kansas, Fire Department Retirement Plan, as originally established by the Overland Park Fire Department, Incorporated, as of January 1, 1969, subsequently amended and restated by the Overland Park Fire Department, Incorporated, and then assumed by the City of Overland Park, Kansas, as of September 1, 2003 (the “Fire Department Plan”), and the Overland Park, Kansas, Fire Department Retirement Trust, as established by the Overland Park Fire Department, Incorporated, and then assumed, amended, and restated by the City of Overland Park, Kansas, as of September 1, 2003 (the “Fire Department Trust Agreement”), and reestablished by Charter Ordinance No. Eighty-Three are hereby reestablished and reaffirmed.

The Governing Body is authorized to amend, by resolution, either the Police Department Plan or Trust Agreement or the Fire Department Plan or Trust Agreement, provided that such amendments shall not conflict with any provisions of this Charter Ordinance.

SECTION 3.

  1. A.
    Police Department Trust Agreement. There shall be five trustees to act as provided in the Police Department Trust Agreement. The Mayor shall appoint three trustees, although the appointment of two trustees shall be with the consent of the City Council. Beginning in 1982, such trustees shall be appointed for designated terms of three years, two years, and one year and until their successors are appointed and qualified. In years after 1982, each appointed trustee shall serve three years and until his or her successor is appointed and qualified.

    Two additional trustees shall be elected from among the participating membership of the Police Department Retirement Plan, by a majority vote of the members of the Plan casting ballots. In 1982, trustees elected by the participating members shall serve for specified terms of two years and three years and until their successors are appointed and qualified. At each future election, a trustee elected by the participating members shall serve for a period of three years and until his or her successor is appointed and qualified.

    The term of each trustee of the Police Department Trust Agreement shall commence on the second Tuesday of April following his or her appointment or election. Other provisions concerning the appointment, election, resignation, or removal of a trustee may be specified in the Police Department Trust Agreement, so long as such provisions do not conflict with any provisions of this Charter Ordinance.

  2. B.
    Fire Department Trust Agreement. There shall be five trustees to act as provided in the Fire Department Trust Agreement. The Mayor shall appoint three trustees, although the appointment of two such trustees shall be with the consent of the City Council. Effective as of September 1, 2003, such trustees shall be appointed for designated terms of three years, two years, and one year and until their successors are appointed and qualified. In years after 2003, each appointed trustee shall serve three years and until his or her successor is appointed and qualified.

    Two additional trustees shall be elected from among the participating membership of the Fire Department Retirement Plan, by a majority vote of the members of the Plan casting ballots. Such trustees in office as of September 1, 2003, shall serve remaining terms of two years and one year and until their successors are elected and qualified. In future years, a trustee elected by the participating members shall serve for a period of three years and until his or her successor is elected and qualified.

    The term of each trustee of the Fire Department Trust Agreement shall commence immediately upon such trustee’s acceptance of his or her appointment or election. Other provisions concerning the appointment, election, resignation, or removal of a trustee may be specified in the Fire Department Trust Agreement, so long as such provisions do not conflict with any provisions of this Charter Ordinance.

SECTION 4. One or more policies providing disability income and life insurance for the benefit of police officers, fire fighters and their dependents shall be purchased and maintained to supplement the provisions of the Police Department Plan, the Fire Department Plan, and their related Trust Agreements.

SECTION 5. For the purpose of funding the Plans and purchasing any insurance described in Section 4 of this Charter Ordinance, the City shall make annual contributions from the General Fund. Said contributions shall be made in compliance with K.S.A. 2002 Supp. 12-5002 or any

amendments thereto, and shall be maintained in order to fund the Plans on an actuarially-sound basis.

In addition thereto, funds shall be utilized for the Plans and said insurance which are obtained by gifts, grants, bequests, gratuities and proceeds from lost or stolen securities, money or personal property which shall remain unclaimed in the possession of the City for six months, together with the proceeds of all unclaimed or confiscated property of any nature which shall have been in the possession of the City for a period of six months, and the City is hereby authorized to sell at public auction any such lost, stolen or unclaimed property.

SECTION 6. Payments upon retirement, discharge, disability, or death to any member or beneficiary of the Police Department Plan or the Fire Department Plan shall be established and paid out in accordance with the terms of the Plans and the Trust Agreements.

SECTION 7. Existing Overland Park Charter Ordinance Number Eighty-Three is hereby repealed.

SECTION 8. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in the Overland Park Sun, an official City newspaper.

SECTION 9. This is a Charter Ordinance and shall take effect sixty-one (61) days after final publication unless within 60 days of its final publication a petition signed by a number of electors of the city of overland park equal to not less than ten percent of the number of electors who voted at the last preceding regular city election shall be filed in the office of the city clerk of overland park demanding an election on the charter ordinance in which case the charter ordinance shall become effective only if and when approved by a majority of the electors voting thereon.

PASSED by the Governing Body not less than two-thirds of the members-elect voting in favor thereof the 7th day of January, 2008.

Carl Gerlach, Mayor

ATTEST:

Marian Cook

City Clerk

APPROVED AS TO FORM:

Michael R. Santos

Deputy City Attorney

Passed: January 7, 2008

January 17, 2008

Effective: March 18, 2008

Charter Ordinance No. Ninety-Three

A CHARTER ORDINANCE EXEMPTING THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS, FROM THE PROVISIONS OF K.S.A. 12-4202, K.S.A. 12-4203 (2007 Supp.), K.S.A. 12-4204, K.S.A.12-4205a, K.S.A. 12-4206, AND K.S.A. 12-4207 AND ANY AMENDMENTS THERETO, PROVIDING SUBSTITUTE AND ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS ON THE SUBJECT MATTERS CONTAINED IN THESE STATUTORY PROVISIONS THROUGH THE ENACTMENT OF AN ORDINARY ORDINANCE, AND REPEALING OVERLAND PARK CHARTER ORDINANCE NUMBERS FORTY-FOUR AND SIXTY.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK,

KANSAS:

SECTION 1. The City of Overland Park, Kansas, by the power vested in it by Article 12, Section 5 of the Constitution of the State of Kansas hereby elects to exempt itself and make inapplicable to it the provisions of K.S.A. 12-4202, K.S.A. 12-4203 (2007 Supp.), K.S.A. 12-4204, K.S.A.12-4205a, K.S.A. 12-4206, and K.S.A. 12-4207 and any amendments thereto and provides for substitute and additional provisions as hereinafter set forth in this Charter Ordinance. The above referenced statutory provisions are part of the Kansas Code of Procedure for Municipal Courts, an enactment of the legislature which is applicable to the City of Overland Park but not uniformly applicable to all cities.

SECTION 2. The Governing Body shall enact, by ordinary ordinance, substitute and additional provisions related to the subject matters contained in the statutes being chartered from.

SECTION 4. This ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in an official City newspaper.

Charter Ordinance No. Ninety-Five

A CHARTER ORDINANCE EXEMPTING THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS FROM THE PROVISIONS OF K.S.A. 12-4108 WHICH RELATE TO THE APPOINTMENT OF THE CLERK OF THE MUNICIPAL COURT AND THE OBLIGATIONS OF THE CLERK OF THE MUNICIPAL COURT IN CARRYING OUT THE DUTIES AND RESPONSIBILITIES OF THE MUNICIPAL COURT AND PROVIDING FOR THE ENACTMENT OF SUBSTITUTE AND ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS PERTAINING TO THE ADMINISTRATION OF THE MUNICIPAL COURT AND THE DUTIES AND RESPONSIBILITIES OF COURT PERSONNEL THROUGH THE ENACTMENT OF AN ORDINARY ORDINANCE.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK, KANSAS:

SECTION 1. The City of Overland Park, Kansas by the power vested in it by Article 12, Section 5, of the Constitution of the State of Kansas, hereby elects to exempt itself from and make inapplicable to it provisions of K.S.A. 12-4108 relating to the appointment of the clerk of the municipal court and the duties, responsibilities and authority of the clerk of the municipal court in carrying out the functions of the municipal court and providing for the enactment of substitute and additional provisions on the same and related subjects through the enactment of an ordinary ordinance. Such referenced statutory provisions are applicable to this City but are not uniformly applicable to all cities.

SECTION 2. The Governing Body shall establish as it deems appropriate and necessary to the effective administration and operation of the municipal court, the procedures for the appointment of a court administrator, court clerk and other such related positions and provide for the duties, responsibilities and authority of such positions and other matters related to carrying out the functions and obligations of the municipal court through the enactment of an ordinary ordinance.

SECTION 3. This Ordinance shall be published once each week for two consecutive weeks in an official City Newspaper.

SECTION 4. THIS IS A CHARTER ORDINANCE AND SHALL TAKE EFFECT 61 DAYS AFTER FINAL PUBLICATION UNLESS WITHIN 60 DAYS OF ITS FINAL PUBLICATION A PETITION SIGNED BY A NUMBER OF ELECTORS OF THE CITY OF OVERLAND PARK EQUAL TO NOT LESS THAN TEN PERCENT OF THE NUMBER OF ELECTORS WHO VOTED AT THE LAST PRECEDING REGULAR CITY ELECTION SHALL BE FILED IN THE OFFICE OF THE CITY CLERK OF OVERLAND PARK DEMANDING AN ELECTION ON THE CHARTER ORDINANCE, IN WHICH CASE THE CHARTER ORDINANCE SHALL BECOME EFFECTIVE ONLY IF AND WHEN APPROVED BY A MAJORITY OF THE ELECTORS VOTING THEREON.

PASSED by the Governing Body, not less than two-thirds of the members elect voting in favor thereof, the 24th day of September, 2012.
